# Plan review handoff — f-20261106-07 (cache and bound the Linux desktop apt install in CI)

* **Plan:** `tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md` (reviewed in PLAN-ONLY mode on BASE 49ec3d142c3043f2411702707af922a34d1ec257).
* **Finding:** f-20261106-07 (ci-workflows, entry build). The co-located f-20261106-02 was not planned, because planner modes do not plan joiners.
* **Executor:** claude (read-only lens leaves through `leaf-launch.sh`). Orchestrator: Claude Opus 5.5, drain session 34dfb924-33cd-4f6c-b00e-034f82dc3f74.
* **Round 1 lens set (every plan-capable lens):** review-plan, review-minimalism, review-correctness, review-error-handling, review-tests, review-platform-semantics, review-tauri-security, review-chess-semantics, review-engine-protocol, review-ipc-contract, review-persisted-state, review-pgn-index. review-code-quality and review-root-cause carry `plan-review: false`.
* **Later rounds:** closure rounds with review-plan plus the witnesses of open issues (r2: +correctness, error-handling, tests, platform-semantics, minimalism; r3: +correctness, error-handling, tests, minimalism; r4: +correctness, error-handling, tests, with a focused PR-4 judgment; r5–r7: +error-handling, tests).
* **Raw evidence:** lens prompts (`lens-<lens>-r<N>.prompt`), JSONL streams, extracted reports (`.txt`) and plan snapshots (`plan-r<N>.md`), published by the supervisor's evidence mirror from the run's RUN_TMP. Container probe outputs and `actions/cache`/toolkit source excerpts are quoted under Traced premises and in the round tables.
* **Decided autonomously (record at adoption through `findings.py record-decision`):** see the plan's "Decisions and trade-offs". Cache plus a per-job network bound, rather than bound-and-retry or a separate test budget. Unify the three copies of the apt install, with `release.yml` excluded.
* **Carried to diff review:** CR-1..CR-5, verbatim in the plan's "Carried to diff review" section. Copy them into every phase brief and diff-review packet.
* **Release-time obligations named by the plan:** the O4 CI release gate (first run saves, the next run shows a set-prefix restore, `Need to get 0 B` and no save) before the finding closes. The Sunday 2026-11-08 `mutation.yml` backend run is a follow-up observation only.

### Round 3 (r3, 2026-11-06) — closure round: review-plan, correctness, error-handling, tests, minimalism
Verdicts: review-plan REVISE · review-correctness REVISE (blocker D1) · review-error-handling APPROVED (should-fix findings) ·

Closed by every witness in r3: PR-11, PR-12, PR-19, PR-25, PR-26, PR-27, PR-28. Reopened: PR-4, its second failed closure.

**PR-4 lineage judgment (second failed closure, build §4 escalation).**
- Invariant: a cache-layer failure never fails the step.
- Previous answers: r2 "a failing restore, key derivation, prune or save degrades to the plain download" had no mechanism. r3 "a failing verification or prune empties the archive directory" was wrong for prune. Prune runs after the download, so emptying the directory left nothing for the `--no-download` install (`E: Unable to fetch some archives`, second probe).
- New evidence: third probe — a stale extra `.deb` in the archive directory does not disturb a `--no-download` install.
- Judgment: simplest correct mechanism, by phase. A failure before the download (verification, key) empties the directory and the download refills it. A failure after the download (prune) keeps every file and skips the save. Restore and save are guarded on a non-empty key with `continue-on-error: true`.
- Changes: O2 Fail-open, probe item 5. The round-4 review-plan prompt carries this as a focused fresh-context judgment request.

| ID | Claim | Witnesses | Disposition | Correction | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Probe item 5 also injects verification and empty-key failures | corrected r4 |
| PR-33 | A corrupt exact-key entry is refetched every run and never healed; no log line | error-handling r3 (2), correctness r3 LATE#2, minimalism r3 limitation | Fix | O2 Save — save whenever the download fetched anything, under a unique key (set prefix + run id + attempt). The set-prefix restore picks the newest entry. One log line per deleted file. Probe items 3 and 4 | corrected r4 |

### Round 4 (r4, 2026-11-06) — closure round plus PR-4 focused judgment: review-plan, correctness, error-handling, tests
Verdicts: all four APPROVED. Raw reports: `$RUN_TMP/lens-<lens>-r4.txt`.
- **Focused judgment (review-plan, fresh context):** the phase-split fail-open model is the simplest correct mechanism. No phase can fail the step or poison a later run. Its one limitation is that the verify/prune fail-open is anchored only by the disposable probe.
- Closed by every witness: PR-33, PR-34, PR-35, PR-36, PR-37. PR-4 is closed by review-plan, correctness and error-handling. review-tests still holds it NOT CLOSED, for two missing verification anchors (PR-47 and PR-48 below, lineage root PR-4). The mechanism itself is no longer disputed.

- Issues: 64 IDs, PR-1..PR-65 (PR-24 was never allocated). 30 Fix closed by a lens. 21 Fix arbiter-closed as nits or reference edits. 5 Fix carried to diff review as CR-1..CR-5 (PR-42, PR-43, PR-45, PR-54, PR-63). 7 Skip (PR-6 partial, PR-17, PR-32, PR-46, PR-56, PR-57, PR-58). 1 duplicate of a carried item (PR-65 = CR-2). 0 Defer. 0 withdrawn. Open at the end: 0.
- Correction-created defects: PR-4 r3 (the prune-empties-directory contradiction, created by the r3 correction of PR-4) and PR-49 r6 (the dead save leg, created by the r5 correction of PR-49). Each was escalated on its second failed closure: a source trace or disposable probe, then a focused fresh-context review-plan judgment.
- Measured premise corrections: `--print-uris` prints MD5Sum, not SHA256 (probe 1). apt 3.2 does not re-hash a same-size cached archive (probe 2, PR-25). The default archive directory hides packages from the resolve (probe 3, PR-19, PR-34). `actions/cache` v4 annotates restore and save failures itself (source trace, PR-49).
- Machine-readable evidence note: `plan-meta.json` lists only issues with a witness the supervisor's manifest parser reads, meaning a finding line that begins with `[blocker|should-fix|nit]`. Many lens reports prefixed findings with `- ` or `**`, which the parser does not read. Those issues are complete in the tables above and in `reviews-handoff.md`.

      {
        "kind": "blocker",
        "text": "tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:79-87 — The plan does not say what `actions/cache` saves and restores. It only says \"the archive directory\", and it hands apt a custom `Dir::Cache::Archives`. apt runs under `sudo`, so it creates `<dir>/lock` (root, mode 0640) and `<dir>/partial` (owned by `_apt`, mode 0700) and writes the `.deb` files as root. `actions/cache/save` runs as the runner user. If its `path` is the directory, `tar` hits \"Permission denied\" on those two entries and exits 2. The save action reports that as a warning, so no entry is ever written and every run stays cold. The plan also treats a failed save as a warning, so this would not turn the job red. The O4 probe uses a PATH shim for `sudo` and `apt-get`, so it cannot produce root-owned files and cannot see this. Only the post-push CI check would notice. The mandate fix (O2) would silently do nothing, and the plan's own \"Need to get 0 B\" check would be the first signal. The plan must name the cache path as `<dir>/*.deb` (not the directory). Alternatively, it must have the step make the saved files runner-readable before the save. The O4 probe should also assert that the path excludes `lock` and `partial`. (confidence: 80)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:90-96 — O3 says `apt-get update` and the download \"run under `timeout`\", but it does not say which side of `sudo` the `timeout` sits on. With `timeout 18m sudo apt-get …`, the runner user's SIGTERM goes to the root-owned `sudo` process. An unprivileged user cannot signal a process that `sudo` has made root, so the kill fails with EPERM and `timeout` keeps waiting. The stall then continues until the job limit, which is the original failure. Only `sudo timeout …` bounds the real command. The O4(a) probe sleeps past the bound using a shim for `sudo`. That shim is not setuid, so the probe passes whichever way the executor writes it, and the defect reaches CI undetected. The plan should specify `sudo timeout`, or run the bounded apt calls from a root shell. It should also require the probe to cover real `sudo` ordering, or state that it cannot. (confidence: 80)"
      }
    ],

    "artefact": "lens-error-handling-r1.jsonl",
    "findings": [
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O2 (`## Approach`, \"Order: apt-get update → resolve → restore → download → prune → save → install\") — The plan states a fail direction only for a save-reservation conflict (a warning). The key resolve, restore, hash, prune and key-derivation steps have no stated direction. The cache is an optimisation, so every one of them should fail open to the plain bounded mirror download. Under errexit and pipefail, an unguarded failure in any of them turns the job red inside the setup step, a failure source this change adds. On the scheduled `mutation.yml` `backend` and `catalog-engines.yml` Linux legs nobody reruns, so a cache-infrastructure failure there is a red scheduled run with no recovery. The plan also does not say that an empty `--print-uris` output is ambiguous: it can mean \"all packages already installed\" or \"resolution failed\". Only the apt exit status tells them apart. State fail-open for every non-download cache step, and require an exit-status check on the resolve step. (confidence: 85)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O3 (\"The non-network `apt-get install` from the local archive … stays unbounded\") — The install is only non-network if the archive is complete. If the resolved set differs from the install-time resolution, or a download step ends short, plain `apt-get install -y` silently fetches from the mirror with no bound. That is the original defect again, in the one step the plan leaves unbounded, and in `rust` it only ends at the job timeout. Specify `--no-download` on the install, or put it under the same bound. A missing archive then fails fast and by name inside the setup step, rather than stalling. (confidence: 80)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O3 (input `timeout-minutes`, \"default empty = no bound\") and O4(a) — The input's failure semantics are unspecified. Empty means unbounded, and `timeout 0` also means unbounded, so a typo or an evaluated `0` silently removes the bound that is this task's deliverable. The probe's `0.05` also cannot go through shell integer arithmetic, and the plan does not say how fractional minutes are converted. Require validation: a non-empty value that is not a positive number fails the step before any apt call. Add that case to the probe. (confidence: 80)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O3/O4(a) — Nothing pins that the bound actually stops the real process tree. The form `timeout … sudo apt-get`, versus `sudo timeout --kill-after=… apt-get`, is unspecified. An unprivileged `timeout` signalling a root `sudo`/`apt-get` and its `_apt` method children is the case most likely to leave the step hanging after the bound. That hang would end at the job limit inside the same step, which the push skill would treat as a timed-out setup step. The probe uses a sleeping PATH shim, which cannot show the signal propagation, and CI never runs the timeout path, only the hit path. Pin the root-owned `timeout --kill-after` form. Extend probe (a) to use a shim that ignores SIGTERM and assert the step returns at about the bound. I could not trace apt's own SIGTERM handling and treat it as a named limitation. (confidence: 80)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "nit",
        "text": "plan O3 (`::error::` annotation) — The plan names only the expiry case. It does not say that a non-timeout apt failure, such as exit 100 or a 404, must not be labelled \"mirror stall\". Probe (a) tests only the timeout path. The annotation should also name the phase (`update` or `download`) and say whether the bound is shared across both commands. \"Total network time\" is stated, but per-command `timeout` calls would allow twice the bound. Add a probe case for a non-timeout apt failure. (confidence: 80)"
      }
    ],

### lens-chess-semantics-r1.txt

NOT APPLICABLE: the plan changes only CI workflow and composite-action YAML (`.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`, `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`, `test.yml`, `mutation.yml`, `catalog-engines.yml`). It caches or bounds the apt download of the desktop build dependencies. It touches no game tree, move path, FEN comparison, ply parity, variation ordering, or PGN and header round trip, so none of this lens's invariants can be violated.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-correctness-r1.txt

Round-1 correctness review of the plan. I found two defects. Both sit at the boundary between what apt does as root and what the plan's other steps assume. I read the plan, the current `setup-ci`, the three workflows, the push skill's rerun exception (SKILL.md lines 411-415), and the gate script `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`. I did not read `.claude/rules/*.md`, `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or the evidence logs under `/tmp/.../ev/`, because the sandbox denied that access. Nothing here rests on those files.

[blocker] tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:79-87 — The plan does not say what `actions/cache` saves and restores. It only says "the archive directory", and it hands apt a custom `Dir::Cache::Archives`. apt runs under `sudo`, so it creates `<dir>/lock` (root, mode 0640) and `<dir>/partial` (owned by `_apt`, mode 0700) and writes the `.deb` files as root. `actions/cache/save` runs as the runner user. If its `path` is the directory, `tar` hits "Permission denied" on those two entries and exits 2. The save action reports that as a warning, so no entry is ever written and every run stays cold. The plan also treats a failed save as a warning, so this would not turn the job red. The O4 probe uses a PATH shim for `sudo` and `apt-get`, so it cannot produce root-owned files and cannot see this. Only the post-push CI check would notice. The mandate fix (O2) would silently do nothing, and the plan's own "Need to get 0 B" check would be the first signal. The plan must name the cache path as `<dir>/*.deb` (not the directory). Alternatively, it must have the step make the saved files runner-readable before the save. The O4 probe should also assert that the path excludes `lock` and `partial`. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:90-96 — O3 says `apt-get update` and the download "run under `timeout`", but it does not say which side of `sudo` the `timeout` sits on. With `timeout 18m sudo apt-get …`, the runner user's SIGTERM goes to the root-owned `sudo` process. An unprivileged user cannot signal a process that `sudo` has made root, so the kill fails with EPERM and `timeout` keeps waiting. The stall then continues until the job limit, which is the original failure. Only `sudo timeout …` bounds the real command. The O4(a) probe sleeps past the bound using a shim for `sudo`. That shim is not setuid, so the probe passes whichever way the executor writes it, and the defect reaches CI undetected. The plan should specify `sudo timeout`, or run the bounded apt calls from a root shell. It should also require the probe to cover real `sudo` ordering, or state that it cannot. (confidence: 80)

**Limitations (below the reporting threshold, not defects I could prove):**
- **Budget arithmetic (about 55):** O3 gives update and download one shared budget. If the remaining time is computed in whole seconds or minutes and reaches 0, `timeout 0` disables the bound. A negative value makes `timeout` exit 125 with an invalid-duration error. Update finishing within the last second of the bound would leave the download unbounded. The plan should require "remaining ≤ 0 means fail now".
- **Expression syntax (about 60):** The key and restore prefix are written as `${{ runner.os }}-${ImageOS}`. `ImageOS` is a runner environment variable. It is not in the `env` expression context unless something sets it. It has to come from a shell step output, or `restore-keys` would end up with an empty segment.
- **Gate script:** `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1088-1096` requires `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` literally in `setup-ci/action.yml`. The plan keeps that condition on the call to `linux-desktop-deps`, so this holds only if the executor keeps that exact text.
- **Timeout sizing:** The 18-minute bound leaves about 1–2 minutes of margin in a 30-minute job. I did not mentally execute the sizing beyond the plan's own measurements.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-correctness-r2.txt

**Correctness lens, round 2.** I found no blocker. I read the plan body, the full delta, all four rule files, `setup-ci/action.yml`, the three call sites and the routing-test contract. I could not run `apt-get --print-uris` here because the sandbox denied it, so the apt-behaviour claims rest on the plan's recorded container probe and on what I know of apt.

**(1) Open issues**

- PR-1 CLOSED — O2 "What the cache holds" caches only the `*.deb` files. Save and restore are unaffected by the root-owned `lock` or the 0700 `partial/`.
- PR-2 CLOSED — O4 now runs the composite's real `run:` bodies as a non-root user with real `sudo` and apt in a container, plus a CI release gate. The shim probe is gone.
- PR-3 CLOSED — O4 "Static" states that `workflows:check` covers workflows only and adds `shellcheck` on the extracted bodies.
- PR-4 CLOSED — O2 "Fail-open" separates an apt failure (step fails) from a cache or key failure (fail-open).
- PR-5 CLOSED — O1 keeps the literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` on the calling step. That is the string pinned at `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1094`. The `.github/actions/**` frontend-mutation input glob (`scripts/run-frontend-mutation.mjs:50`) also covers the new action file.
- PR-7 CLOSED — The key hashes sorted `filename size checksum` columns without the URI. A different mirror host gives the same key.
- PR-9 CLOSED — `sudo timeout --kill-after=<grace> <remaining> apt-get …` signals as root. Exit codes 124 and 137 match `timeout`'s behaviour.
- PR-10 CLOSED — The key and prefix come from step outputs, with codename and dpkg arch replacing `ImageOS`. The `env` context would not carry runner variables.
- PR-11 CLOSED — The install uses `--no-download`, so a missing archive fails by name.
- PR-12 CLOSED — An empty value means no bound, and a non-positive or non-numeric value fails before any apt call. Probe item 7 covers it.
- PR-14 CLOSED — The wiring contract checks the `rust` input, the `setup-ci` forward, both new callers and the absence of inline installs. `readCiWorkflowTexts` currently reads only `test.yml`, `mutation.yml` and the `setup-ci` action. The executor must add `catalog-engines.yml` and the new action file. That is an obligation, not a plan defect.
- PR-15 CLOSED — The CI gate names exact criteria: `cache-hit` true, `Need to get 0 B` in both jobs, confirmed by the orchestrator before closure. `test.yml` has `workflow_dispatch`, so the dispatch route works.
- PR-16 CLOSED — `catalog-engines.yml` has `defaults.run.shell: bash` and a Linux-only guard. Both it and `mutation.yml` `backend` check out before the local action. The composite declares `shell: bash` explicitly.

**(2) Defects introduced by the delta**

- [should-fix] plan O2 "Download set" vs "Archive directory" — The text hands the new archive directory to apt for the download and the install, but not for the `--print-uris` resolve. If the runner's default `/var/cache/apt/archives` already held a valid `.deb` for a needed package, the resolve would omit that package from the set and the key. The download into the fresh directory would still fetch it. The prune would then delete it, because its name is not in the set, and `--no-download` would fail the install. Resolving with the same `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=<fresh dir>` removes the coupling. The mandate's logs show full 63.7 MB fetches, so this is probably latent. I could not run apt to check it (confidence: 55, below threshold).
- [nit] plan O3 "Annotation" — Exit 137 is also what an OOM-killed apt returns, so it would be labelled a mirror stall. Comparing elapsed time against the bound would separate the two (confidence: 50).
- [nit] plan O4 probe item 6 — A proxy that accepts connections and never answers is cut off by apt's own 120-second idle timeout. That produces a non-timeout failure if the probe's bound is longer than 120 seconds, and the item would not show the `timeout` path (confidence: 55).

The other delta hunks hold up:
- The sizing arithmetic is 18 + ≤10 = 28 minutes against the 30-minute budget. Adding a cold Rust cache is named in Risks.
- Saving only when `cache-hit != 'true'` is consistent with partial `restore-keys` hits, which report `cache-hit` false.
- The save-before-install ordering is correct. The prune set comes from the pre-restore resolve, which is complete.

**(3) Blockers elsewhere in the plan**

None.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] plan O3 "Shared deadline" — The text does not say whether the deadline is wall-clock or the sum of command durations. The `actions/cache` restore, which has its own 10-minute segment timeout, is not under the bound. A stalled cache restore combined with a stalled mirror could still exceed the job budget. This is contrived and covered by the Risks wording (confidence: 40).

**Limitations:** I did not execute apt, `sudo timeout` or `actions/cache` semantics. Those branches are covered only by the plan's probe record and by my reading of apt and `timeout` behaviour.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-correctness-r3.txt

**Limitations.** I could not read the global `CLAUDE.md` rules 4, 6d, 11 and 12b, because the file tools are confined to the worktree and the plans directory. I could not run any apt probe, and the composite action does not exist yet, so shell-level behaviour is judged from the plan text only. I could not verify the "about 75 minutes" claim for `frontend` either, because the run logs in `$RUN_TMP` are not available to me.

## (1) Open issues

- PR-4 [blocker] NOT CLOSED. The fail-open mechanism contradicts the step order and the `--no-download` install. See D1 below.
- PR-11 CLOSED. The second probe shows `--no-download` with a missing `.deb` fails with `E: Unable to fetch some archives` and fetches nothing. O3 and probe item 6 match that.
- PR-12 CLOSED. Probe item 8 covers `0`, `-1`, `abc`, `1.5` and the empty value, and the plan states the empty-means-unbounded rule. The setup-ci default for `desktop-timeout-minutes` must be `""`, which O3 implies.
- PR-19 CLOSED. O2 now resolves against the same empty archive directory, so the set no longer depends on `/var/cache/apt/archives`. Verification and prune both work from the full set, so prune cannot delete a package that `--no-download` needs.
- PR-25 CLOSED. The second probe measured that apt 3.2.0 accepts a same-size corrupted `.deb` (`Need to get 0 B`), and the threat model is corrected. The new "Verify restored files" step compares size and checksum against a set derived from the signed index, which covers that case. The checksum-algorithm mapping (MD5Sum/SHA256/SHA512 to the right tool) is left to the executor, and probe item 1 on 26.04 will show the real format.
- PR-26, PR-27, PR-28 CLOSED, with no correctness mismatch. Extending `readCiWorkflowTexts` with extra keys is compatible with the rejection loop's `texts[file].replaceAll(...)` and `ciWorkflowContracts(changed)`. The pinned literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` stays in `setup-ci`.

## (2) Defects introduced by the DELTA

**D1.** [blocker] Plan O2 "Fail-open" and "Prune", with O3 "Install" and probe item 5 (confidence: 85).
- **The contradiction.** O2 orders the steps as download, prune, save, install. The r3 mechanism says "A failing verification or prune empties the archive directory, so everything is downloaded, and the step continues."
- **Verification is coherent.** It runs before the download, so emptying the directory just means a full download.
- **Prune is not.** It runs after the download. If it fails (say `find -delete` errors) and the handler empties the directory, nothing is left to install. The install is now `--no-download`, so it fails with `E: Unable to fetch some archives`. That contradicts "never fails the step" and probe item 5's "ends the step with exit 0 after a full download".
- **No way to get the stated result.** Nothing in the plan reruns the download after prune, so the case cannot reach exit 0 as written.
- **Needed fix.** Either leave the directory untouched on a prune failure (stale `.deb`s are harmless to apt, and only the cache bound is lost), or say explicitly that a failed prune empties the directory and reruns the download within the deadline. The first option is simpler.
- **Effect.** This r3 mechanism combined with the r2 `--no-download` fix turns a fail-open path into an install failure. The probe item written to prove it cannot pass.

## (3) Other blockers

None beyond D1. The remaining obligations are consistent at their boundaries:
- **Source anchors.** The premises' line anchors match the current sources, including `setup-ci` lines 31–41, `mutation.yml` 74–81, `catalog-engines.yml` 89–97 and the `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` pins.
- **Job sizing.** The `rust` sizing (18 + ≈1 + ≤10 = 29 ≤ 30) holds against the measured 8–10 min total.
- **Composite and caller wiring.** A local composite called from `setup-ci` and from the `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` jobs is valid, because checkout precedes it in all three. Windows and macOS legs skip the step through the `if`.
- **Exact-key save condition.** "Save only when the exact key was not restored" is well-defined, as long as the executor tests `cache-hit != 'true'` and not `== 'false'`. A pure miss leaves the `actions/cache` output empty.

## LATE OBSERVATIONS

- [nit] O3 Annotation: the label requires "elapsed time at or past the bound". The executor should compute remaining and elapsed from the same integer epoch source. A mix of fractional and truncated clocks could leave a genuine exit 124 unlabelled by under a second (confidence: 40, below the reporting threshold).
- [nit] O2 Save: when the exact key restores but verification deleted and refetched a corrupt file, the save is skipped. The bad entry then costs a refetch every run until it is evicted. This is rare and only costs time (confidence: 45).

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-correctness-r4.txt

(1) Open issues

- PR-4 CLOSED. The phase-based mechanism holds.
  - Verification and key derivation run before the download. Their failure leaves an empty or partial directory that the download refills.
  - Prune runs after the download. A failed prune keeps every file and skips the save.
  - The third probe shows a stale extra `.deb` does not disturb `--no-download`, so the earlier "prune empties the directory, install fails" contradiction is gone.
  - Restore and save are guarded on a non-empty key and carry `continue-on-error`. A `--print-uris` exit failure stays loud on purpose.
  - Probe item 5 now covers prune failure, verification failure and empty key.
  - I found no other cache-layer operation that can fail the step. Apart from the LATE OBSERVATIONS, the plan leaves only the executor-owned requirement that key derivation inside the resolve step must not fail that step while the apt exit status does.
- PR-33 CLOSED.
  - Save now triggers on "download fetched ≥1 file" under a unique key (set prefix + run id + attempt).
  - Set-prefix restore picks the newest entry, so a healed set supersedes a corrupt one.
  - A run that downloads nothing saves nothing. This is consistent with probe items 3 and 4 and with the CI gate's "no save".
  - The `frontend` and `rust` jobs share run id and attempt, so they compute the same save key. The loser gets a reservation warning, which the plan already allows.
- PR-34 CLOSED. The resolve and the download must pass the same `Dir::Cache::Archives`. The third probe (0 lines against the default directory, 1 line against an empty one) justifies the seeded-default check in probe item 1. The resolve-before-restore order in O2 keeps the directory empty at resolve time.
- PR-35 CLOSED. Pinning the literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"` is scoped to the `rust` job body. `with:` in `test.yml` is the shape the existing `desktop: "true"` already uses.
- PR-36 CLOSED. Probe item 9 covers SIGKILL and a 404. Both give an exit code (137, or 100) with elapsed time below the bound, so the label condition ("124/137 and elapsed ≥ bound") correctly withholds the stall label.
- PR-37 CLOSED. There are exactly two apt network commands, update and download. `--print-uris` reads only local lists, and restore is a `uses:` step. A per-command pin therefore covers the whole network surface.

(2) Defects introduced by the DELTA

None at blocker or should-fix level. Checks against the repo:
- The `scripts/run-frontend-mutation.mjs:50` claim is correct. `.github/actions/**` is in the mutation inputs set, so the new action forces the full mutation run on the pushed SHA.
- The `catalog-engines.yml` path-filter addition fits the file. Both `push.paths` and `pull_request.paths` exist, and the workflow already lists itself. The Linux guard `if: runner.os == 'Linux'` sits on the step that becomes the action call. Checkout precedes it.
- The `mutation.yml` `backend` job also checks out before the call.
- `frontend` and `rust` have no `needs` edge, so the "same key, one saves" gate is a real race. The plan already treats the loser as a warning.
- The 18 + 1 + 10 = 29 minute budget arithmetic holds.
- The CR-3 integer-epoch note is correct. Remaining is computed from the floored start, so the real expiry is at or after the deadline and elapsed is at least the bound.

(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None.

LATE OBSERVATIONS

These are unexecuted hazards, not demonstrated defects, and both are below the 80 threshold.
- [nit] O2 Archive directory (plan, delta-adjacent) — The plan says the directory is "world-traversable, created by the runner user" but does not say its path is fixed. `actions/cache` derives the cache version from the `path` pattern strings. A random `mktemp -d` path would give a different version each run, so restore would never match. Probe items 1–9 simulate the cache by copying files and would not catch this. Only the post-push CI gate would. I recommend naming a fixed path in O2 (confidence: 60).
- [nit] O2 Download set — "The restore's primary `key` is the unique save key, which never matches" is imprecise. `frontend` and `rust` share the same run id and attempt, so a job that restores after the other has saved gets an exact hit. The effect is benign: the files are verified, nothing is fetched, nothing is saved. It does mean the first run's gate can show `cache-hit: true` in one job (confidence: 45).
- [nit] O4 probe item 3 — A second run in the same container sees the packages already installed. The resolve then prints an empty set and skips the cache steps. The probe needs a fresh container with the saved `.deb` set restored (confidence: 55).

Limitations: I did not execute apt or `actions/cache`. The `actions/cache` key-matching and version behaviour above comes from my knowledge of the toolkit, not from this repository. The rule files `async-resource-invariants.md` and `gate-tooling.md` have no clause that conflicts with the plan's CI-only changes.

### lens-error-handling-r1.txt

Two checks I couldn't complete first, then the findings.

- **Context I couldn't read:** `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md`, global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (rules 4, 6d, 11, 12b) and the evidence logs under `/tmp/drain-plan-drain-90a2aa88-slot0/build/ev/` are outside the directories this session may read. The Read tool refused them and Bash was denied. I used the plan, the in-worktree rule files, `SKILL.md:390-420` and the three workflow files instead. I did not trace the plan's cited timings against the logs.
- **`shell: bash` in composite actions:** this runs with errexit and pipefail, so any unguarded failure in a new cache-bookkeeping command fails the whole step. Finding 1 depends on that.

**Findings**

[should-fix] plan O2 (`## Approach`, "Order: apt-get update → resolve → restore → download → prune → save → install") — The plan states a fail direction only for a save-reservation conflict (a warning). The key resolve, restore, hash, prune and key-derivation steps have no stated direction. The cache is an optimisation, so every one of them should fail open to the plain bounded mirror download. Under errexit and pipefail, an unguarded failure in any of them turns the job red inside the setup step, a failure source this change adds. On the scheduled `mutation.yml` `backend` and `catalog-engines.yml` Linux legs nobody reruns, so a cache-infrastructure failure there is a red scheduled run with no recovery. The plan also does not say that an empty `--print-uris` output is ambiguous: it can mean "all packages already installed" or "resolution failed". Only the apt exit status tells them apart. State fail-open for every non-download cache step, and require an exit-status check on the resolve step. (confidence: 85)

[should-fix] plan O3 ("The non-network `apt-get install` from the local archive … stays unbounded") — The install is only non-network if the archive is complete. If the resolved set differs from the install-time resolution, or a download step ends short, plain `apt-get install -y` silently fetches from the mirror with no bound. That is the original defect again, in the one step the plan leaves unbounded, and in `rust` it only ends at the job timeout. Specify `--no-download` on the install, or put it under the same bound. A missing archive then fails fast and by name inside the setup step, rather than stalling. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan O3 (input `timeout-minutes`, "default empty = no bound") and O4(a) — The input's failure semantics are unspecified. Empty means unbounded, and `timeout 0` also means unbounded, so a typo or an evaluated `0` silently removes the bound that is this task's deliverable. The probe's `0.05` also cannot go through shell integer arithmetic, and the plan does not say how fractional minutes are converted. Require validation: a non-empty value that is not a positive number fails the step before any apt call. Add that case to the probe. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan O3/O4(a) — Nothing pins that the bound actually stops the real process tree. The form `timeout … sudo apt-get`, versus `sudo timeout --kill-after=… apt-get`, is unspecified. An unprivileged `timeout` signalling a root `sudo`/`apt-get` and its `_apt` method children is the case most likely to leave the step hanging after the bound. That hang would end at the job limit inside the same step, which the push skill would treat as a timed-out setup step. The probe uses a sleeping PATH shim, which cannot show the signal propagation, and CI never runs the timeout path, only the hit path. Pin the root-owned `timeout --kill-after` form. Extend probe (a) to use a shim that ignores SIGTERM and assert the step returns at about the bound. I could not trace apt's own SIGTERM handling and treat it as a named limitation. (confidence: 80)

[nit] plan O3 (`::error::` annotation) — The plan names only the expiry case. It does not say that a non-timeout apt failure, such as exit 100 or a 404, must not be labelled "mirror stall". Probe (a) tests only the timeout path. The annotation should also name the phase (`update` or `download`) and say whether the bound is shared across both commands. "Total network time" is stated, but per-command `timeout` calls would allow twice the bound. Add a probe case for a non-timeout apt failure. (confidence: 80)

The plan's no-retry choice and its reliance on the push skill's single `gh run rerun --failed` are consistent with `SKILL.md:411-415`. The step surfaces as "Set up repository dependencies" at `test.yml:249`, which matches the exception. I found no retry-idempotency defect.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-error-handling-r3.txt

**Limitations.** I could not read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or the global CLAUDE.md rules (4, 6d, 11, 12b). The sandbox confined file access to the worktree and the plans directory, and a Bash call was denied. I judged from the lens text, the plan body, the DELTA, `gate-tooling.md`, `async-resource-invariants.md`, push `SKILL.md` lines 390–425 and the current `setup-ci/action.yml`. Plan line numbers below refer to the r3 body snapshot.

## (1) Open issues

- `[should-fix]` **PR-4 NOT CLOSED** (plan:121-122, plan:182). The restore and save steps now have a real fail-open mechanism: they are guarded on a non-empty key and carry `continue-on-error`. The sentence "A failing verification or prune empties the archive directory, so everything is downloaded" is wrong for prune.
  - The stated order (plan:91) is download → prune → save → install, so prune runs after the download.
  - A prune failure that empties the directory deletes the packages just downloaded. The `--no-download` install then fails with `E: Unable to fetch some archives` (second probe, plan:70-71). A cache optimisation again turns a working install into a setup failure, which is the PR-4 defect.
  - Probe item 5 (plan:182) demands "exit 0 after a full download" after a prune failure, but no second download exists in the order. The executor must invent one outside the deadline, or the probe cannot pass.
  - The correct fail-open for prune is to keep the downloaded files and skip the save. The plan must say that, because an unpruned set would carry stale entries into the exact-key entry.
  - Whether the emptying itself can fail, and what happens to a corrupt file left behind, is also unspecified.
  - Confidence: 85.
- **PR-11 CLOSED.** The O3 Install bullet is `--no-download`. The probe measured a fast failure with no fetch (plan:70-71). Probe item 6 and the O4 text pin cover it.
- **PR-12 CLOSED.** O3 Input fails a non-positive or non-numeric value before any apt call. Probe item 8 asserts `0`, `-1`, `abc`, `1.5` and the empty value.
- **PR-19 CLOSED.** The resolve runs against the same, still empty, archive directory. The download set is therefore deterministic, so prune plus `--no-download` cannot lose a package because of the runner's default cache.
- **PR-25 CLOSED.** The same-size corruption claim was measured on apt 3.2.0, and the threat model now states that apt checks size only. O2 "Verify restored files" and probe item 4 cover it. See the new finding below about its silent and non-healing behaviour.
- **PR-26 CLOSED.** The O4 wiring contract pins the cache steps with `continue-on-error`, save before install, `sudo timeout --kill-after`, and `--no-download`, all scoped to step text.
- **PR-27 CLOSED.** Probe item 7 covers expiry in the update phase, expiry in the download phase, and a deadline already used up before the download. It also keeps the probe bound under apt's 120 s idle timeout.
- **PR-28 CLOSED.** The rejection-table rows and the extended `readCiWorkflowTexts` give a concrete mechanism for "goes red when reverted".

## (2) Defects introduced by the DELTA

- `[should-fix]` plan:109-111, plan:115-116 — The new "Verify restored files" step deletes a corrupt restored `.deb` silently and re-downloads it from the slow mirror. The Save rule is unchanged: "Only when the exact key was not restored". On an exact-key hit with a corrupt entry, the following happens on every run:
  - The file is re-downloaded at about 40 kB/s.
  - Nothing is saved, so the corrupt entry is never replaced.
  - No log line says a file was dropped.

  The slow-mirror cost this change exists to remove returns undetected until the package set changes. The plan should require one log line per deleted file (name, expected and actual size or checksum) and say how a corrupt exact-key entry is healed, or accept it as a known limit. Only the first release-gate run would expose it. Confidence: 80.
- `[should-fix]` (same as PR-4 above) The delta's "Fail-open" mechanism sentence (plan:120-122) is where the prune contradiction was introduced.

None.

- A hung `actions/cache` restore cannot be bounded in a composite step. It still lands in the setup step, so it matches the push skill's rerun exception.

### lens-error-handling-r4.txt

I've read the plan, the delta, the rule files I could reach and the current workflows. I'm writing up the findings now.

Two limits on what I could check:
- The global `CLAUDE.md` and `review-lens-contract.md` sit outside the allowed directories. I judged from the repo rules and the plan's own stated policy: the cache fails open, and the deadline fails closed.
- Bash was denied for `git` after a `cd`, so I used the file tools only.

## (1) Open issues

- `PR-4` **CLOSED**.
  - The failure direction is now specified by phase and holds on each path I traced.
  - A failure before the download (verification, key) empties the archive directory, and the download refills it.
  - A failure after the download (prune) keeps every file, which the third probe shows is safe for a `--no-download` install. The save is skipped, so an unpruned set is never cached.
  - Restore and save are guarded on a non-empty key with `continue-on-error: true`. A truncated restore is caught by the size check.
  - This round's gap is that the degradations are silent. I file it as the new `NEW-1` below rather than reopening `PR-4`.
- `PR-33` **CLOSED**.
  - The save now runs whenever the download fetched at least one file, under a unique key (set prefix plus run id and attempt). The newest-entry restore picks up the healed set.
  - Each deleted file gets a log line.
  - I found no new swallow. A repeatedly failing save only costs a re-download, and the plan keeps it a warning.
- `PR-34` **CLOSED**. Probe item 1 seeds the default archive directory, and the wiring contract pins the same `Dir::Cache::Archives` on both the resolve and the download. A revert is now visible.
- `PR-35` **CLOSED**. Pinning the literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"` means an emptied value fails the contract.
- `PR-36` **CLOSED**. Probe item 9 (SIGKILL or a 404 before the bound) exercises the "non-zero before the deadline, no stall label" branch. It gives 137 with elapsed under the bound, so it is not mislabelled.
- `PR-37` **CLOSED**. One `sudo timeout --kill-after` match per network command means losing it from the download is caught.

## (2) Defects introduced by the delta

**[should-fix] NEW-1 plan O2 "Fail-open" — the degraded paths never surface to the run (confidence: 82)**
- Key derivation, verification and prune each end in "degrades to the plain download", "keeps every file and skips the save" or "empties the directory". The plan says only the per-file mismatch in verification gets a log line.
- None of these paths requires a `::warning::` or a job-summary line naming the stage and reason. In the log, a degraded run looks the same as a clean miss. The only record of it is a red-x `continue-on-error` step inside a collapsed composite.
- If a prune or verify bug, or an `if:` guard naming the wrong output, disables the cache, every run quietly pays the full mirror download. That is the original f-20261106-07 failure, now with no trace.
- The O4 release gate checks the cache only once, at closure.
- **Fix:** require one `::warning::` line on each fail-open branch, naming the stage and the reason. Optionally add a one-line "apt cache: hit | partial | miss | degraded" summary per run, and add the warning to the probe assertions in item 5.
- Trace limit: I could not see the planned action body, so I judge the contract text only.

**[should-fix] NEW-2 plan O2 "Save" and "Fail-open" — the save guard rests on cross-step state the plan never names (confidence: 80)**
- "Skip the save after a prune failure" and "save only if the download fetched at least one file" both need a later step to read a flag. A step output cannot be changed after the step that set it, so prune and download must each emit a new output.
- The plan names no such output, and the wiring contract pins only that the save step exists and comes before the install.
- If the detection step itself errors, the intended direction is unstated. Skipping the save is the safe direction. Saving after a failed prune is the one the plan forbids ("an unpruned set is never cached").
- The probe harness runs the `run:` bodies and reproduces `GITHUB_OUTPUT`. It does not evaluate GitHub `if:` expressions, so "skips the save" is proven only up to the output value.
- **Fix:** state that a failed or undetermined fetched-count or prune result means "do not save", and pin the guard's output name in the wiring contract.

## (3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None.

- **[nit] O3 "Input" (confidence: 65)** — an invalid `timeout-minutes` fails the step, but the plan does not say the message names the bad value. Only a deadline expiry gets the `::error::` annotation.
- **[nit] `mutation.yml` `backend` (confidence: 55)** — its eight matrix jobs will emit up to seven "Unable to reserve cache" warnings on a cold run. These are benign warnings the plan already accepts.

### lens-error-handling-r5.txt

**(1) Open issues**

- PR-47 CLOSED — O2 now guards key derivation separately from the `--print-uris` exit status. A derivation failure empties the key output and prints a warning, and apt's own exit status still fails the step. Probe item 5 injects a broken hash command rather than a hand-set empty output. That exercises the real `set -e` path, and the contract is fail-open to a full download with exit 0.
- PR-48 CLOSED — O4 pins the step-scoped `if:` guards with rejection rows. The save guard is key non-empty, fetched and prune ok. A loosened save guard (cache an unpruned set) therefore fails the contract. The fail direction is pinned as "no save" on any weakening. The step splitter that scoping needs is already carried as CR-1.
- [should-fix] PR-49 NOT CLOSED — O2 "Every degradation is visible" promises one `::warning::` for each of five branches. Three of them (key derivation, verification, prune) are `run:` bodies, so a plan-authored warning line is possible, and probe item 5 asserts it. The other two, restore and save, are `uses:` steps under `continue-on-error: true`, and a `uses:` step cannot print a plan-authored warning.
  - The plan names no follow-up step that reads `steps.<restore|save>.outcome == 'failure'` and prints the warning.
  - Probe item 5 runs only `run:` bodies, so it cannot assert those two lines.
  - The O4 wiring contract pins the `if:` guards and `continue-on-error`, but no warning step.
  - What a failed restore or save then shows depends only on `actions/cache`'s own annotation. I have not traced that internal behavior, so it is a named limitation (confidence 60). The plan's stated property ("a run that degraded never looks like a clean miss") has no mechanism for two of its five branches.
  - Correction: name an outcome-reading warning step for each `uses:` step, and add it to the wiring contract with a rejection row. Or narrow the claim to the three `run:` branches and say the restore and save branches rely on `actions/cache`'s own annotation.
  - (confidence: 82)
- PR-50 CLOSED — O2 "Save guard state" names both pieces of cross-step state, the download step's fetched output and the prune step's ok output. The save `if:` needs all three values to be present and true. A missing or undetermined value means no save. That is the safe direction, because the cost is one extra cold download and never a poisoned entry.

**(2) Defects introduced by the DELTA hunks**

None above threshold beyond PR-49. I checked these:
- **Restore `key` equals the save key.** An exact hit can only come from a parallel job of the same run. That job saved only a set that had already been downloaded and pruned. A same-key reservation conflict on save is a warning and never fails the job. Rerun attempts get a new key because the attempt number is part of it.
- **Fixed archive path.** Hosted runners are fresh VMs, and verification and prune handle stale files. No new failure path.
- **Probe items 3 and 8 and CR-4.** They tighten verification and carry no failure-semantics gap.

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None. I re-read the O3 fail-closed path against the push skill's rerun exception (`SKILL.md:411-415`). The deadline expiry fails inside "Set up repository dependencies" with a labelled `::error::`. Non-timeout apt failures keep apt's own status. There is no in-job retry, and the cache writes use unique keys, so a rerun is idempotent.

I could not read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or the global `CLAUDE.md` rules 4, 6d, 11 and 12b. The file tools are confined to the worktree and the plans directory. I judged fail direction from the plan's own stated policy, the push skill lines and the repo rules instead.

### lens-error-handling-r6.txt

**(1) Open issues**

- [should-fix] PR-49 NOT CLOSED: the r6 correction makes restore failures visible, but the save half of the mechanism can't fire. The plan cites `saveImpl.ts:76` → `utils.logWarning` → `core.info('[warning]…')`. That means a failed save never calls `setFailed`, so `steps.<save>.outcome` stays `success` (confidence: 85). The new step "prints a `::warning::` for each that is `failure`", so for a reservation conflict or upload error it prints nothing. The plan's own citation therefore contradicts the claim that the save outcome step covers save failures. The degraded save still shows in the log as plain `[warning] …` text from `actions/cache`. So the real surface is that text, not an annotation. Probe item 5 hand-sets the save outcome to `failure` to test the step. That is the same defect the plan already rejected for key derivation ("a broken hash command, not a hand-set empty output"). It proves the shell branch and says nothing about whether the condition can occur. Fix: drop the save outcome from the claim and the wiring pin, and state that save failures surface only as `actions/cache`'s `[warning]` log text. Or find a signal that actually changes on a failed save. Keep the restore outcome check, since `restoreImpl.ts:88` does call `setFailed`.
- PR-59 CLOSED. The same fixed `path:` literal is pinned in both cache steps, and a `mktemp` creation of that directory is rejected. This closes the silent "restore never matches" degradation from a path change. The CI release gate (the next run must show `Need to get 0 B`) backs it up end to end.
- CR-3 and CR-5 remain carried to diff review. I have no new evidence on them. Both are consistent with fail-closed labelling and the literal `== 'true'` guard.

**(2) Defects introduced by the DELTA**

- [nit] Plan O2 "Every degradation is visible": the outcome-warning step has no stated `if:`. It sits after the save, and the save comes after the download. With the default `success()`, a stall or apt failure in the download step skips it. Then a failed restore that forced the full download (the case that led to the stall) is not echoed as a warning. Specify `if: always()` (or equivalent), and state that the step itself never fails. The step is a plain echo, so this is not a blocker (confidence: 80).
- No other delta hunk affects failure semantics. The two further hunks are the three-output emptying and the `cache-hit` note move. Both are correct. The CR-5 and Phase 1 file-list additions are neutral.

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None. The fail directions are consistent and stated:
- The cache is fail-open, with a guarded save and the "undetermined means do not save" rule.
- The bound is fail-closed: invalid `timeout-minutes` fails before any apt call.
- A stall is labelled only on deadline expiry. Other apt failures keep apt's own exit status.
- There is no retry, so there is no retry-safety issue.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

None that I can trace at 80 or higher confidence.

**Limitations**
- Bash was denied, so I could not fetch `actions/cache@v4` sources myself. The PR-49 finding relies on the plan's own cited lines (`saveImpl.ts:76`, `actionUtils.ts:28-30`). I did not trace `@actions/cache` toolkit internals, such as whether `restoreCache` or `saveCache` annotate through `core.warning` themselves (confidence below 80, not reported).
- The global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` rules 4, 6d, 11 and 12b were unreadable because the file tools are confined to the worktree. I judged against `gate-tooling.md`, `async-resource-invariants.md` (bound and cleanup on every exit path) and `push/SKILL.md` lines 395–420 only.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-minimalism-r1.txt

NO FINDINGS at confidence ≥ 80.

**Minimum-diff counterfactual.** The smallest change that meets the mandate's "identified setup failure" outcome is a `timeout` wrapper in the `setup-ci` apt step, which is a few lines. That alone would not meet the plan's goal of avoiding the stall, because a rerun hits the same slow mirror. So the cache is justified. The plan's measurements show the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled master runs. The mandate also lists "cache the desktop apt packages" as an option.

**Checked and found necessary:**
- **New `linux-desktop-deps` action (O1).** The package list exists in four places: `setup-ci/action.yml:31-41`, `mutation.yml:74-81`, `catalog-engines.yml:89-97` and `release.yml`. The plan keeps `release.yml` separate because its pins differ. Adding the cache to only one copy would leave the others uncached, so extracting to a shared action matches the "extract at the second copy" rule. It has three callers, so it is not a single-caller abstraction. Folding in `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` is consolidation of a pre-existing duplicate, which this lens puts in scope. The mandate's "(or another desktop job)" wording covers it.
- **`timeout-minutes` input and its `desktop-timeout-minutes` pass-through in `setup-ci` (O3).** There is one real non-default value (18, for `rust`), and a fixed bound would wrongly cap the 90-minute `frontend` job. `rust` reaches the action only through `setup-ci`, so the pass-through input is unavoidable.
- **Separate `actions/cache/restore` and `actions/cache/save` steps.** A plain `actions/cache` saves in its post step and skips the save when the job failed. That is the exact scenario here, so the split is needed.
- **No in-job retry.** The plan rejects it, which keeps the diff smaller.
- **O4 probe.** It is not committed, so it adds nothing to the diff.

**Named limitations (not defects):**
1. **Cache-key machinery (confidence 55 that a smaller design suffices).** The key is a hash of `apt-get install --print-uris` output, plus a fallback prefix, plus a prune step to stop carried-forward entries growing. The mandate's own suggestion was only "keyed on the package list". A cheaper variant would key on `hashFiles(action.yml)` plus `${ImageVersion}`, with no fallback prefix and no prune. After an image bump the first job would pay a full cold download, and between bumps a stale hit would re-download the changed packages every run without re-saving. I can't tell from the evidence which one is better. If the executor can show the print-uris, hash and prune path is under about 25 lines of shell, I'd leave it as is. If it grows beyond that, the simpler key is the better trade.
2. **`::error::` annotation with elapsed time (confidence 50 it is unneeded).** The push skill's rerun exception matches on the failing step's name, not on an annotation. This is a few lines and does not move the verdict.
3. **Coverage gap (not a defect).** I could not read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or the global CLAUDE.md rules 4, 6d, 11 and 12b. The file tools are confined to the worktree and the plan directory, and a combined Bash command was denied. `.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md` has no minimalism or magic-number text, so the "magic value" rule could not be checked against project rules. The `18` bound is justified in the plan's sizing, so I expect it to pass a named-constant check.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-minimalism-r2.txt

Status: I read the plan, the delta, `setup-ci/action.yml`, the existing CI wiring contract in `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` and the push skill's rerun exception. Nothing else needed reading. Global CLAUDE.md rules 4, 6d, 11 and 12b, and `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md`, could not be read because the sandbox denied access. I judged those rules only through the plan's own citations of them. That is a missing-context limitation.

**(1) Open issues**

- PR-1 CLOSED — caching only the `.deb` files is the minimal fix, since the root-owned `lock` and 0700 `partial/` make the directory unreadable to `actions/cache/save`. It adds no extra construct.
- PR-2 CLOSED — the real-apt probe is disposable and adds no committed diff. It replaces the shim probe rather than adding to it.
- PR-3 CLOSED — one `shellcheck` pass over the extracted bodies.
- PR-4 CLOSED — fail-open is stated once in O2 and not repeated per step.
- PR-5 CLOSED — the one literal condition is kept, and it is the text the existing contract already pins.
- PR-7 CLOSED — the key drops the mirror URI, which removes churn instead of adding code.
- PR-9 CLOSED — `sudo timeout --kill-after` is the minimum form that can signal root.
- PR-10 CLOSED — step outputs are required because `actions/cache` takes its key as an expression.
- PR-11 CLOSED — one flag, `--no-download`.
- PR-12 CLOSED — a few lines of validation. See the limitation below.
- PR-14 CLOSED — the checks use the file's existing `requireText`/`forbid` style. See the limitation below.
- PR-15 CLOSED — this is a verification gate, not extra diff.
- PR-16 CLOSED — `shell: bash` is required, and the Linux guard already exists.

**(2) Defects introduced by the delta**

None at or above 80 confidence.

The delta text is mostly clarification of constructs that r1 already had. It adds two things beyond that: the `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` wiring contract, and `--no-download` plus the input validation. All three trace to a named correction.

I checked the main minimum-diff counterfactuals and none is deletable:

- **Bound-only (no cache).** This would cut about 70% of the diff and satisfies the mandate's "identified setup failure" literally. The plan rejects it on measurements: the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled runs, so the mandate's failure scenario (the next push refused) would recur after the single allowed rerun. The cache is grounded in the mandate, so this is not a defect.
- **The 4-way unification.** The package list is copied into four places (`setup-ci`, `mutation.yml` `backend`, `catalog-engines.yml` and `release.yml`). Extracting it for three callers follows the extract-at-second-copy rule, and leaving two uncached copies would defeat the mandate. `release.yml` correctly stays out because its pins differ.
- **A simpler key.** A static key from the package list would never refresh, because cache entries are immutable. The key has to come from the resolved set, so the resolve step is necessary.
- **`desktop-timeout-minutes` pass-through.** Only `rust` uses it, but it is the mechanism the mandate needs. It is not speculative.

Named limitations. These are below the reporting threshold, so they are not defects:

- The wiring check "no workflow except `release.yml` keeps an inline `apt-get install` of `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev`" overlaps the check that `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` call the action. Its extra value is catching a brand-new workflow that reintroduces the copy. That would also need a new `catalog-engines.yml` read and a directory scan in a file that currently reads three texts. I can't tell whether it earns its keep (confidence 60).
- Positive-number validation of `timeout-minutes` guards an input whose only caller passes a literal `"18"`. The "empty means unbounded" design is what creates the hazard it plugs, and it is about three lines (confidence 55).

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] Plan O2 — the restore-keys fallback prefix is the only reason the prune step and the "carried-forward entries" paragraph exist. Dropping both would shrink O2 by a step and an output. The cost is a full 64 MB download after each package-set change on a slow mirror. I can't tell how often the set changes (confidence 60, below threshold).
- [nit] Plan O3 — the shared deadline across update and download needs a start time carried across the cache-restore step, remaining-time arithmetic and phase naming. The plan's own measurements show `apt-get update` takes 0–31 s. Bounding only the download would be simpler. PR-13 closed the doubled-bound concern, so I'm not re-raising it (confidence 55, below threshold).

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-minimalism-r3.txt

**Missing context.** The file tools are confined to the worktree and the plans directory. I could not read the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (rules 4, 6d, 11, 12b) or `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md`. I used the plan's own citations of rules 6d and 11, plus `.claude/rules/async-resource-invariants.md:34-36` ("the second approximately similar implementation gets extracted"). I read `gate-tooling.md` and `SKILL.md:395-420` in full.

## (1) Open issues

Each is judged on this lens only: is the correction the smallest sufficient one for the MANDATE?

- PR-4 CLOSED — O2 Fail-open uses a guard on a non-empty key plus `continue-on-error: true`. That is a mechanism, not a new layer.
- PR-11 CLOSED — `--no-download` is one flag, and the probe measured the behaviour.
- PR-12 CLOSED — input validation is one check, and the extra probe cases are disposable, not committed.
- PR-19 CLOSED — resolving against the same empty archive directory is one flag. It adds no step.
- PR-25 CLOSED — the verify step is small, and I found no cheaper form that still covers the measured same-size case. Whether it is needed at all is raised under Named limitations (confidence 60, below the cut-off).
- PR-26 CLOSED — the composite-internal pins add rows to an existing table. No new machinery. Their value is raised under Named limitations (confidence 55, below the cut-off).
- PR-27 CLOSED — the extra probe cases are disposable and add nothing to the committed diff.
- PR-28 CLOSED — the directory-wide scan I flagged in r2 is gone. The plan now checks the two fixed files and extends `readCiWorkflowTexts` by two paths.

## (2) Defects introduced by the DELTA

None at or above the 80-confidence cut-off. Checked against this lens:

- **Second copy (item 6).** The extraction at the second copy is intact. `setup-ci`, `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` all route through `linux-desktop-deps`. The delta added no new copy. `release.yml` stays separate because its package list and pins differ.
- **Pass-through layers (item 3).** The `desktop-timeout-minutes` forward in `setup-ci` has one consumer, `rust`. It cannot be inlined, because composite steps accept no `timeout-minutes` at the call site. It is not a pass-through defect.
- **Speculative extension points (item 4).** The delta adds no new configuration or flag. The one new condition, elapsed time at or past the bound before labelling an OOM kill as a stall, is a single comparison. PR-20 was arbiter-closed.
- **Instrumentation (item 5).** The delta adds no logging, metrics or retry. Its only new error handling is the fail-open mechanism, which is required behaviour.

## (3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None.

## Named limitations

These are below the reporting threshold. I did not turn them into defects.

- **Verify-restored-files step, O2 (confidence 60 it is deletable).** The threat model scopes the step to "corruption only" but names no realistic source of same-size corruption. Saves happen only after a successful download, so a truncated file already changes size and apt refetches it. `actions/cache` also transports the entry. The only evidence is a synthetic four-byte overwrite. The case for keeping the step is that a corrupted exact-key entry would fail every rerun until eviction. I could not tell whether that outweighs the extra step, so I'm not reporting it as a defect.
- **Composite-internal text pins, O4 (confidence 55 they exceed the MANDATE).** Four string pins (`continue-on-error: true`, save before install, `sudo timeout --kill-after`, `--no-download`) guard implementation text rather than behaviour. They go beyond the cross-file wiring pins: the `rust` input, the `setup-ci` forward, and the two callers with no inline install. The real-apt probe and the CI release gate already prove the behaviour once. The smallest alternative keeps only the cross-file pins. The pins' value is stopping a later edit from silently undoing the bound. The MANDATE does not ask for that.
- **Prune and its failure handling (PR-32, already Skip).** The delta adds an "empty the directory on prune failure" clause and probe item 5, so prune now costs a little more than in r2. I'm not re-raising PR-32. The prefix fallback still needs prune to bound growth, and I have no evidence the extra cost changes that.

**Minimum-diff counterfactual:** a restore/save of the `.deb` set keyed on the resolved download set, `rust` bounded at 18 minutes, and one shared action for the three callers. Dropping the verify step and the four composite-internal pins would remove roughly 10–15 lines of shell and 4–5 table rows. I did not find that a substantial share of the diff.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-plan-r1.txt

Findings are below. I read the plan, the three workflows, `setup-ci`, the push skill's rerun exception and the scripts that pin these files. File tools are confined to the worktree and Bash refused my compound command, so I could not open `/tmp/.../ev/job-*.log`, and I ran no real apt probe.

**[blocker] PLAN.md:106-117, 157-158 — O4 would not go red if the cache mechanism were wrong.**
- The only pre-commit proof is a probe that shims `apt-get` and `sudo`. A shim cannot test the claims the plan rests on:
  - apt omits already-present archives from `--print-uris` and honours `-o Dir::Cache::Archives`;
  - apt verifies cached `.deb` files against the index and reports `Need to get 0 B`;
  - `actions/cache` can save and restore the directory.
- Most likely to fail: apt runs under `sudo` and creates root-owned `lock` and `_apt`-owned 0700 `partial/` inside the "runner-writable" archive directory (PLAN.md:79-81). The runner-user tar in `actions/cache/save` can then hit permission errors. Per PLAN.md:86-87, a failed save is only a warning, so the job stays green and the cache is never populated. The probe cannot show this, and no check fails.
- Probe item (a) shims `sudo`, so it also hides whether `timeout` actually stops a real `sudo apt-get`.
- The cache-hit evidence is the "CI, after the push" bullet, which Phase 1 defers to "collected at release". It is outside the proof that gates the phase.
- If the mechanism is wrong, `rust` and `frontend` go red on the next push. `ci:remote:check` then blocks the following push, which is the failure scenario in MANDATE.
- The plan needs proof against real apt, or a gating CI observation of a cache save followed by a hit, that fails when the cache does nothing. (confidence: 80)

**[should-fix] PLAN.md:157 — `pnpm workflows:check` does not lint the new composite action.**
- `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:297-304` enumerates only direct children of `.github/workflows`, and `:343-351` runs actionlint with no arguments, so it covers only `.github/workflows/`.
- `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml` holds nearly all the new logic: key derivation, prune, timeout and error handling. It gets no static check.
- O4's "Static" bullet (PLAN.md:107-108) therefore overstates what it covers. (confidence: 75)

**[should-fix] PLAN.md:71-87 — O2/O3 give no failure semantics for the cache layer itself.**
- Only a failed save reservation is declared non-fatal.
- A non-zero `--print-uris`, an unparseable or empty download set, a prune error or a restore error is not specified as degrading to the old plain `apt-get install`.
- Today's install works. Without fail-open semantics, an optimisation layer can turn it into a setup failure. The reversal path (PLAN.md:131-132) is a manual edit. (confidence: 65)

**[should-fix] PLAN.md:152-156 — a consumer that pins `setup-ci` text is not named.**
- `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1094` requires the literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` inside `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`. `gates:routing:test` runs it from `gates:contract:check`.
- It stays green only if that `if:` remains in `setup-ci` on the step that calls the new action. The plan does not say so.
- The other pins are unaffected: `scripts/run-frontend-mutation.mjs:50` already covers `.github/actions/**`, and no test pins the `mutation.yml` or `catalog-engines.yml` apt steps. (confidence: 60)

**[should-fix] PLAN.md:71-77, 89-104, 110-113 — over-specification below the obligations.**
- The exact key string composition, the `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=…` flag, the "0.05-minute" bound and the (a)/(b)/(c) probe cases are executor detail.
- Move them to the phase brief and keep the contracts: the key derives from what apt would fetch, a stall fails inside the setup step, and the cache is bounded. (confidence: 55)

**[nit] PLAN.md:74-77 — the key hashes the whole `--print-uris` output, including mirror URIs.**
- If the runner picks a different mirror host or scheme between runs, the exact key never matches.
- Probe (b) only checks reordering and a changed SHA256, not URI variance. The key probably should use filename, size and SHA256 only. (confidence: 40, `Skip` by the sub-80 rule)

**[nit] PLAN.md:97-101 — the 18-minute bound only narrows the MANDATE failure mode.**
- The sizing (18 + 1 + ≤10) rests on three warm-Rust-cache runs. A push that cold-builds Rust, combined with a download just under 18 minutes, still times out in a test step.
- The "Risks" section mentions only the cold-cache setup failure, not this case. (confidence: 50, `Skip`)

**Named limitations**
- I could not read the evidence job logs.
- I could not verify the runner image's apt configuration, such as `Acquire::Retries` and the HTTP timeouts. That affects whether a trickling mirror ever trips an apt-level timeout.
- I could not run real `apt-get --print-uris` against the resolute mirror, so the output format and "omits present archives" are unconfirmed (confidence ≈ 70 that both hold).
- I could not verify how `timeout` interacts with `sudo` signal relay. Reported as a risk inside the blocker, not as a separate defect.

**What holds up**
- The setup-step failure does land in the outer "Set up repository dependencies" step, which the push skill's exception at `SKILL.md:411-415` matches.
- The desktop consumers are correctly identified (`test.yml:173` and `:250`; `mutation.yml:74-81` and `catalog-engines.yml:89-97` are standalone copies).
- `release.yml` is correctly excluded.
- The permission checker only restricts `contents: write` jobs, so these read-only jobs are unaffected.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-plan-r2.txt

(1) Open issues, all checked against the r2 candidate

- PR-1 CLOSED: the cache `path` is now the `.deb` files only (O2 "What the cache holds"). apt leaves a root-owned `lock` and a 0700 `partial/` in the directory, which `actions/cache/save` running as the runner user could not read. Using `/tmp` rather than `$RUNNER_TEMP` also avoids the 0750 home-directory traversal problem for `_apt`. Probe item 2 can fail on this.
- PR-2 CLOSED: the PATH-shim probe is replaced by the real composite `run:` bodies run in a container as a non-root user with real `sudo` (items 1–7). The cache-action wiring is covered by the CI release gate. Both can go red.
- PR-3 CLOSED: O4 Static now says `workflows:check` covers `.github/workflows/` only (`scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:288-301`). `shellcheck` runs over the extracted bodies, and `~/.local/bin/shellcheck` exists here.
- PR-4 CLOSED: O2 Fail-open separates an optimisation failure, which degrades to the bounded download, from a non-zero `--print-uris` exit, which is an apt failure.
- PR-5 CLOSED: `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1094` pins `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` in `setup-ci`. O1 keeps that literal on the calling step. The `forbid("composite has no checkout")` pin at line 1090 is unaffected by the nested local action.

### lens-plan-r4.txt

Status: I've read the plan, the delta and the callers it names, and checked them against the code. The last step is writing up the focused judgment on the fail-open model.

**(1) Open issues**

- PR-4 CLOSED, with a named limitation. Phase by phase against the invariant "a cache-layer failure never fails the step":
  - **Key derivation:** an empty key skips restore and save, and the plain download proceeds. The apt resolve failing stays a hard failure by design.
  - **Restore:** it carries `continue-on-error: true`. A truncated or partial extract differs in size, so apt refetches it.
  - **Verification:** it runs before the download. Emptying the directory makes the download refill it.
  - **Download:** this is the bounded mirror path, not the cache layer.
  - **Prune:** it runs after the download. A stale extra `.deb` doesn't disturb the install (third probe, `Traced premises`). The failed prune also skips the save, so an unpruned set is never cached.
  - **Save:** it carries `continue-on-error: true`. Unique keys plus newest-wins restore heal a corrupt entry.
  - **Install:** `--no-download` over a directory that verification, download and prune left complete.
  - **Poison:** no path found. Every file saved was either apt-verified at download time or checked by the verify step against the signed set.
  - **Simplest mechanism:** yes. The split rule is "before the download, empty the directory; after it, keep everything and skip the save". The design already uses `restore-keys` with a never-matching key (`test.yml:346`).
  - **Limitation (confidence 70):** only restore and save are pinned in the committed wiring contract. Verify and prune fail-open rest on the disposable probe item 5. PR-17 and PR-46 already accepted that.
- PR-33 CLOSED. The unique key (set prefix plus run id and attempt) plus the newest-wins prefix restore and the "save whenever anything was fetched" rule heal a corrupt entry. Probe items 3 and 4 can fail.
- PR-34 CLOSED. Probe item 1 seeds the default archive directory, and the `Dir::Cache::Archives` equality pin covers both commands.
- PR-35 CLOSED. The literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"` is pinned.
- PR-36 CLOSED. Probe item 9 covers a failure before the bound (SIGKILL or 404) with no label.
- PR-37 CLOSED. One `sudo timeout --kill-after` match per network command.
- PR-33, PR-34, PR-35, PR-36 and PR-37 were checked against the delta text only. There was no new source evidence, so closure rests on the corrected wording and the probes it names.

**(2) Defects introduced by the DELTA**

None at or above should-fix. The new `catalog-engines.yml` path filter entry needs no routing pin: `git grep` finds no script that pins that workflow's path lists. The full-frontend-mutation expectation matches `scripts/run-frontend-mutation.mjs:50`.

**(3) Blockers**

None.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] PLAN.md O2 Download set — the plan says the restore's primary key "never matches". `frontend` and `rust` in one run share the same `<set prefix><run_id>-<attempt>` key. Whichever job saves first can therefore produce an exact hit for the other job's restore, and the second save conflicts. Both outcomes are benign and already fail-open. The O4 CI gate wording ("one of them saving it") still holds (confidence: 75).
- [nit] PLAN.md O3 Shared deadline — a restore stall of up to the 10-minute `actions/cache` segment timeout is spent from the 18-minute `rust` bound. If the deadline reaches zero, the step fails with the mirror-stall label, although the cause was the cache. It is bounded and is already the arbiter-closed PR-40 wording. Reported only because it is the one place where cache time can shorten the deadline (confidence: 55).
- [nit] PLAN.md O2 Verify — the checksum column is `MD5Sum:` on 24.04 and 26.04, but apt prints whichever hash type its index offers. The executor must dispatch on the type prefix. Probe item 1 ("real format parses") would catch a wrong assumption (confidence: 40).
- [nit] PLAN.md Risks — the `amd64v3` variant pool is host-CPU dependent, so the two desktop jobs, or successive runs, may resolve different sets. Alternation could cause repeated bare-prefix fallbacks, each re-downloading from the slow mirror. No evidence of the frequency, so this stays a nit, covered by "rerun once" in the O4 CI gate (confidence: 35).
