### lens-plan-r6.txt

**Lens: plan review, round 6.** Bash was denied in this session, so I could not fetch `actions/cache@v4` source or use `git log` or `git blame`. I read the repo with Read and Grep. For the `actions/cache` behaviour I used the plan's own cited lines plus my recollection of v4. Confidence figures reflect that.

## (1) Open issues

- **[blocker] PR-49 NOT CLOSED.** `PLAN.md` O2 "Every degradation is visible" (body lines 145-152) and O4 probe item 5 (lines 232-233) claim that a failed save becomes a `::warning::` by reading `steps.<save>.outcome == 'failure'`. The source the plan cites defeats this for the save step.
  - **Why the save leg is dead:** The plan says save failures go through `utils.logWarning` at `saveImpl.ts:76`, and `logWarning` is `core.info("[warning]…")` at `actionUtils.ts:28-30`. That is a `catch` that logs and returns, so the save step finishes with outcome `success` on a failed save. `steps.<save>.outcome` is therefore never `failure`, and the step never prints for save.
  - **What this breaks:** The sentence "A run that degraded never looks like a clean miss in the log" is false for save, which is the case a silent no-cache regression would hit. The O4 wiring pin ("outcome-warning step reads both the restore and save outcomes", line 200-201) locks the dead read in.
  - **Why the proof can't catch it:** Probe item 5 runs the step body with outcomes hand-set to `failure`, so it passes while production can never produce that input. Nothing proves the claim at a level that can fail.
  - **Restore leg:** it is real only for `core.setFailed`-class errors, because the toolkit turns most restore errors into its own `core.warning`.
  - **Fix:** drop the save leg and state that save failures show only as `[warning]` info text. Alternatively, use a signal that can actually fail for save. Either way the proof must exercise the real signal. (confidence: 82)
  - **Smaller point:** the outcome-warning step is placed "after the save" with default `success()` semantics. When the download step fails, which is the stall case, the step is skipped and a restore degradation goes unreported. It needs an `if: always()`-style condition, or the plan should say that gap is accepted. (confidence: 60, should-fix)

- **PR-59 CLOSED.** O4 now pins the same fixed literal in both `path:` fields and forbids `mktemp` for that directory (`PLAN.md` lines 198-200). A `mktemp` revert goes red against a mutated-string row. The residue is in section 2.

Carried CR-1..CR-5 are unchanged and correctly deferred to diff review. PR-60, 61 and 62 are arbiter-closed, and the delta text matches their corrections.

## (2) Defects in the delta, or in obligations that depend on it

- **[should-fix] PLAN.md:198-200 against 118-120 — the PR-59 pin is ambiguous.**
  - O2 says the cache `path` is "the `.deb` files only, never the directory", because root-owned `lock` and `partial/` are unreadable to `cache/save`. The new O4 pin says the `path:` values are "the same fixed archive-directory literal". A builder following the pin literally could set `path: /tmp/linux-desktop-apt-archives`, which is exactly the shape O2 warns against.
  - Nothing ties the `Dir::Cache::Archives` literal in the run steps to the cache `path` literal. O4 only requires that the resolve and download commands agree with each other. If those two drift apart, save finds no `.deb` files and warns only through `core.info`, and the first-run CI gate is the only thing that would catch it.
  - Fix: pin the `<dir>/*.deb` form, and require the same directory literal in the `Dir::Cache::Archives` option. (confidence: 70)

## (3) Other blockers

None found. I re-checked these against the code and they hold:
- **Consumers:** the three callers (`setup-ci`, `mutation.yml:74`, `catalog-engines.yml:89`) match the plan. `readCiWorkflowTexts` and `ciWorkflowContracts` have only the two test consumers in `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`.
- **Pinned text:** `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` is pinned at `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1094`.
- **Permissions:** `mutation.yml` is `contents: read` at workflow level, so unpinned `actions/cache@v4` inside the new composite is allowed. The checker only scans job-level steps in `contents: write` jobs.
- **Push-skill exception:** the composite surfaces as the outer step "Set up repository dependencies", which matches `SKILL.md:411-415`.

## LATE OBSERVATIONS

None beyond the above.

### lens-platform-semantics-r1.txt

I've read the plan and the workflows it touches, and I'm now writing up the findings.

**Platform semantics review of `2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md` (round 1)**

All of this is Linux apt behavior, and the plan changes no Windows or macOS code. The cross-platform risk is the shared `catalog-engines.yml` matrix. Its Windows and macOS legs must stay untouched, and it has a Linux-only step guarded by `if: runner.os == 'Linux'` (`catalog-engines.yml:89`). One command, `gh api …/actions/cache/usage`, was never run by me. I took the cache figures from the plan.

- `[should-fix] plan:76-78 (O2) — The cache key is written as `${{ runner.os }}-${ImageOS}-<hash>`, which mixes two syntaxes.` `${ImageOS}` is bash expansion. `actions/cache` takes `key:` as an expression, so the key has to come from a prior shell step through `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`. Nothing in the repo uses `env.ImageOS` in an expression (`git grep ImageOS` is empty), and I can't confirm the `env` context exposes runner-set variables. If it doesn't, the key silently loses the image component, or the step fails. The plan should say that the resolve step emits the full key and the restore prefix as outputs. `ImageOS` is also redundant, because the hash already covers the distro through the package filenames and versions. (confidence: 60)

- `[should-fix] plan:114-117, 157-158 (O4/Phases) — The runtime proof leaves out the `mutation.yml` `backend` caller.` That job runs only on `workflow_dispatch` or the Sunday 03:17 cron (`mutation.yml:31,50`). A push to master never runs it, so the pushed-SHA evidence covers only `frontend` and `rust`. The `catalog-engines.yml` caller is covered, because editing its own workflow file matches its `push.paths` filter (`catalog-engines.yml:25-26`). Its three matrix legs must still be checked: the Linux leg runs the action, and the Windows and macOS legs skip it and stay green. The plan should add `gh workflow run mutation.yml` for `backend`. The `if: runner.os == 'Linux'` guard must carry over to the `uses:` step. Note too that `defaults.run.shell: bash` does not reach `uses:` steps, so the composite's own steps need explicit `shell: bash`. (confidence: 75)

- `[should-fix] plan:74-77 (O2) — The key is hashed over the whole `--print-uris` output, including the mirror host.` The URI contains the mirror (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com` today). If hosted images use a different mirror, the exact key changes for identical packages, and the cache misses and re-downloads exactly when the mirror is unhealthy. Hash only the filename, size and SHA256 columns, sorted. The (b) probe should assert that the key does not change when only the host changes. (confidence: 65)

- `[should-fix] plan:79-81, 109-113 (O2/O4) — The archive directory must work with apt's `_apt` sandbox, and the probe can't show that.` Download-only runs as root but fetches as `_apt`. A directory under `$RUNNER_TEMP` (`/home/runner/work/_temp`) inside a `0750` home isn't reachable by `_apt`. apt then prints a "Download is performed unsandboxed as root" notice and falls back, so it should still work. The `PATH`-shim probe can't prove that. It also can't prove that `--print-uris -o Dir::Cache::Archives=…` reports the full set against an empty directory, or that `%3a`-encoded filenames in the output match the on-disk names the prune step compares. The plan should specify a world-traversable directory such as `/tmp/...` and run the real `apt-get --print-uris` once on a Debian or Ubuntu host. Otherwise it should name the first CI run as the only proof of those behaviors. (confidence: 55)

- `[nit] plan:90-93 (O3) — The shared `timeout` bound is under-specified for two reasons.` First, one bound covers both `apt-get update` and the download, so the second command needs the remaining budget. The 0.05-minute probe implies fractional minutes, and bash can't compute those without `awk`. Second, use `sudo timeout --kill-after=… <secs> apt-get …` rather than `timeout sudo …`, so the signal reaches apt as root. Check for exit status 124 before printing `::error::`. (confidence: 50)

- `[nit] plan:98-101 (O3) — The 18-minute budget may be too tight on a cold, healthy mirror.` The sum (18 + ~1 + ≤10 = 29 minutes) leaves about 1 minute of slack against the 30-minute job limit. A job that spends the full 18 minutes downloading and then runs the observed 8–10 minutes of work may still hit the job timeout, in a test step. The plan accepts this as a risk, so it is a note only. (confidence: 35)

CI jobs that give runtime proof:
- `frontend` and `rust` on the pushed SHA (Linux).
- All three `catalog-engines.yml` matrix legs on the same push.
- A dispatched `mutation.yml` run, once the plan adds it.

The `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` compile check and the `target_os = "freebsd"` probe don't apply, because no Rust source changes.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-platform-semantics-r2.txt

1. Open issues
- PR-1 CLOSED. The cache `path` is the `.deb` files only (O2 "What the cache holds"). apt leaves root-owned 0644 files in a runner-owned `/tmp` directory. The runner can read them to save and delete them to prune. Probe item 2 checks this.
- PR-2 CLOSED. A real-apt, non-root, real-sudo container probe replaces the shim (O4 items 1–7). A CI release gate backs it, and its pass criteria are exact. See LATE OBSERVATIONS for probe fidelity.
- PR-3 CLOSED. O4 Static says `workflows:check` covers workflows only, and adds `shellcheck` on the extracted composite `run:` bodies.
- PR-4 CLOSED. O2 Fail-open separates cache failures, which degrade to the mirror download, from apt resolve failures, which fail with apt's own error.
- PR-5 CLOSED. O1 keeps the literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'` on the calling step. I checked this against `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1094`, which pins that literal in the `setup-ci` text. The existing "composite has no checkout" forbid applies to `setup-ci` only, so the new action does not trip it.
- PR-7 CLOSED. The key hashes the sorted `filename size checksum` columns and leaves out the mirror URI. Probe item 1 checks that the key is stable across mirror hosts.
- PR-9 CLOSED. `sudo timeout --kill-after` runs the timer as root. GNU `timeout` puts itself and its child in one process group and signals the group, so the `_apt` method children are reached. Probe item 6 uses real sudo.
- PR-10 CLOSED. The key and prefix come from step outputs built from `VERSION_CODENAME` and the dpkg architecture. `ImageOS` is gone.
- PR-11 CLOSED. The install runs with `--no-download`, so a missing archive fails by name instead of starting an unbounded fetch.
- PR-12 CLOSED. A non-positive or non-numeric `timeout-minutes` fails before any apt call, and an empty value means unbounded by design. Probe item 7 checks this.
- PR-14 CLOSED. O4 adds a wiring contract in `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`. `gates:contract:check` runs it from the Ubuntu `frontend` job, and `.gitattributes` has `eol=lf`. Its use of `readFile` with forward-slash `join` paths is not a Windows concern.
- PR-15 CLOSED. The release gate requires `cache-hit` true on the exact key and `Need to get 0 B` in both desktop jobs. The orchestrator confirms this before closure.
- PR-16 CLOSED. The `catalog-engines.yml` call keeps `if: runner.os == 'Linux'`. I checked `catalog-engines.yml:89-97` (Linux-only step, `defaults.run.shell: bash`, checkout first). Every `run:` step in the composite declares `shell: bash`. The Windows and macOS legs skip the step and must stay green, and `rust-windows-test` and `rust-macos-test` are unaffected. The `mutation.yml` `backend` call has checkout before it, runs on `ubuntu-latest`, and is proven by the wiring contract.

2. Defects introduced by the delta
- None at blocker or should-fix level. The `rust` and `frontend` jobs and the `mutation.yml` `backend` job are all `ubuntu-latest`. The only matrix that includes Windows and macOS is `catalog-engines`, and its call is gated to Linux.

3. Blockers elsewhere in the plan
- None.

LATE OBSERVATIONS
- [nit] plan body:148-151 — The probe's `ubuntu:26.04` container may not have the same `sudo` as the runner. Ubuntu 25.10 and later ship `sudo-rs` as the default `sudo`, and runner images can differ in `use_pty` and `secure_path`. Probe item 6, the stall stop at the bound with exit 124 or 137, has no CI proof, because CI only shows cache hits. Record `sudo --version` and `apt --version` in the probe output, and pass any proxy setting through apt options. `sudo` resets the environment, so `http_proxy` would be dropped. (confidence: 35)
- [nit] plan body:84-91 — The `--print-uris` output depends on the host, because apt picks its `amd64v3` variant pool at runtime. The plan says "as booted" without saying so. If hosted runners with different CPU features resolve different variants, the exact key can alternate between two values. Each one's prune then deletes the other's `.deb` files, so the cache churns and still works. The release gate's "same key in `rust` and `frontend`" could also fail intermittently without any wiring fault. The gate should say to re-run once before blaming the wiring. (confidence: 25)

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-tauri-security-r1.txt

**Lens: Tauri security boundaries — plan review r1 (CI desktop apt cache, f-20261106-07)**

The plan has no findings in this lens's classes.

The plan touches only `.github/actions/*` and the three workflows. None of the lens's classes occur: the Tauri backend's credentials and sessions, filesystem authority and recursive mutation, signed-download verification, and renderer-facing diagnostics. I checked the one adjacent surface, which is the CI cache of apt `.deb` files restored into a directory that root-run apt reads.

- **Cache poisoning of restored `.deb`s (no finding).**
  - **Cache scope:** `test.yml` runs on `push` to any branch and on `pull_request`. GitHub scopes those caches so a feature-branch or PR-ref cache cannot be restored on `master`. The plan's claim at lines 26-27 is correct for these triggers.
  - **Release isolation:** `release.yml` does not use the new action or cache key, so the cache cannot reach a `contents: write` job.
  - **Apt verification:** apt is the integrity check, matching the plan's threat model. Cached archives are checked against the size and hash in the signed `Packages` index, and mismatches are re-downloaded. The fallback-prefix restore of an older entry is covered by the same check. The cache key includes the SHA256s from `--print-uris`, so it tracks what apt would fetch.
  - **Residual risk:** none found at the confidence threshold. The O4 probe does not exercise the claim that apt re-downloads a tampered cached `.deb`. Treat that as an unproven assumption, not a defect.
- **Secrets (no finding).** The touched jobs have `permissions: contents: read` and the plan's steps carry no tokens. The `::error::` annotation prints only elapsed time and the bound, so it exposes no secret, local path or URL.
- **Absent classes (limitation).** There is no bearer-token, keyring, session, path-authority, archive-extraction or Minisign flow in this change. I did not review the Rust, TypeScript or manifest code, because the plan does not touch it.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-tests-r1.txt

**Lens: does the verification prove anything — plan review r1**

Two files were off-limits (denied by the sandbox), so some claims below are unchecked:
- `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` could not be read.
- The `/tmp/.../build/ev/job-*.log` evidence logs could not be read, so I did not re-verify the plan's measured timings or the real `--print-uris` output format.

I read the plan, `gate-tooling.md`, `async-resource-invariants.md`, push `SKILL.md` lines 395-420, `setup-ci/action.yml`, `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (CI wiring contracts), `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs` and the workflow step names.

[blocker] tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:106-117 (O4) and :157 (PROOF COMMAND) — The central behaviour has no durable assertion that goes red on revert. The behaviour is that `rust` is bounded at 18 minutes through `desktop-timeout-minutes` → `setup-ci` → `linux-desktop-deps`, and that the three callers use the shared action.
- The only checks aimed at this behaviour are the O4 probe, which is explicitly "disposable … not committed", and CI evidence "collected at release". Neither is a repeatable gate. Someone could later delete `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`, misspell the forwarded input, or paste inline `apt-get` back into `mutation.yml` or `catalog-engines.yml`. Every committed test would stay green until the next mirror stall.
- The proof command `pnpm workflows:check && pnpm gates:contract:check` is the wrong level for this change. `pnpm gates:contract:check` includes `gates:routing:test`, which pins CI wiring in `ciWorkflowContracts` (`scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1075-1096`). It requires the `uses: ./.github/actions/setup-ci` step and `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`, but nothing about the timeout input or the shared action.
- `workflows:check` runs actionlint with default arguments, which lints only `.github/workflows/`. Nobody lints the shell in the new `linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`.
- A misspelled `${{ inputs.desktop-timeout-minutes }}` inside a composite action also passes. It evaluates to empty, which the plan defines as "no bound", so the job silently goes back to unbounded.
- The O4 probe runs "the action's shell bodies with a PATH shim". By construction it skips the expression wiring across the three files, which is where the defect would hide.
- Required: add the wiring contract to `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`, following the existing `requireText`/`forbid` style:
  - the `rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes`;
  - `setup-ci` forwards it to `linux-desktop-deps`;
  - `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` call `linux-desktop-deps`;
  - no `apt-get install … libwebkit2gtk` remains outside `linux-desktop-deps` and `release.yml`.
- Also commit the key, prune and timeout probes as a script test run by `gates:contract:check`, or name the check that stands in for it. This is necessary verification work for the mandate, not scope expansion. (confidence: 85)

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:109-113 (probes b and c) — The probes assert against canned `--print-uris` text the plan author wrote, not output from the real runner (Ubuntu 26.04, apt 3.x).
- The format the key hash and prune parse is `'URL' filename size SHA256:…`.
- A parser that works on canned text but misreads the real format would give an empty download set. The probe stays green, the key becomes the hash of nothing and never changes, and the prune would delete every `.deb` or none.
- The probes also do not cover the error path of `apt-get install --print-uris -qq` failing or returning nothing. The step must fail loudly there, not cache under a constant key.
- Required: capture real `--print-uris` output from a job log or container as the probe fixture, and add an assertion that empty or failed resolution is an error. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-11-06-ci-desktop-apt-cache.md:114-117 (CI evidence) — The only check that the cache works end to end is vague, manual and not a gate. "One of them saving it" and "Need to get 0 B (or only a small delta)" have no pass or fail threshold.
- The key is a hash over output that includes URIs. If the mirror host or URI form varies between runs, every run misses and saves a new roughly 64 MB entry. That churns the 11.86 GB cache and evicts other entries. No job goes red, and the probes cannot see it.
- Name an exact pass criterion, such as "second run: exact-key restore and the download step fetched 0 packages". Hash only filename and SHA256 so mirror variance cannot change the key, and say who confirms it and when. (confidence: 80)

Limitations (below the reporting threshold, not scored):
- Probe (a) shows `timeout` kills a shimmed `sleep`. It does not show that `timeout` killing `sudo apt-get` leaves the step failing within the bound. Consider `--kill-after`.
- The integrity claim that apt re-verifies a cached `.deb` against the index is asserted in the threat model with no test of a corrupted cached file.
- The rerun-exception match is sound. The failing step is named `Set up repository dependencies` at `test.yml:249`, and the exception is prose in the skill, not code.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-tests-r2.txt

Status: I've read the plan, delta, rule files and the existing wiring-contract code, and I'm writing up the findings. Two gaps: the global lens contract and CLAUDE.md sit outside my sandbox, and my last two `git grep` calls were denied. I relied on the Read, Grep and Glob results for everything below.

## (1) Open issues

- PR-1 CLOSED. Probe item 2 checks that the saved `.deb` set is readable by the non-root user. The release gate (second-run `cache-hit`) goes red if the save never populates.
- PR-2 CLOSED. The real-apt probe has seven items, and the release gate has exact criteria. The probe is uncommitted by design (PR-17 is Skip).
- PR-3 CLOSED. The O4 Static bullet states that `workflows:check` covers `.github/workflows/` only and adds `shellcheck` on the extracted bodies.
- [should-fix] PR-4 NOT CLOSED. O2 "Fail-open" claims that a failed restore, key derivation, prune or save "never fails the step". No probe item and no wiring check exercises that path. Probe item 7 covers only the `--print-uris` non-zero branch. The empty-set, exit-0, "no cache steps run" branch is also unanchored. Making the cache steps hard-fail (for example, dropping `continue-on-error` on the cache steps, or letting a prune error abort the step) keeps every listed assertion green. Add probe items that inject a restore, prune and save failure and assert the step still ends 0, and a text pin for `continue-on-error: true` on the cache steps.
- PR-5 CLOSED. O1 keeps `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`, so the existing `requireText` at `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1094` stays green. The new "forwards under that if" check must be scoped to the step, not a whole-file `includes`. The literal is already in the file, so an unscoped check would pass even if the forward were removed.
- PR-7 CLOSED. Probe item 1 changes only the mirror host and expects the same key. If the hash included the URI again, item 1 would go red.
- [should-fix] PR-11 NOT CLOSED. Probe items 3 and 4 still pass if `--no-download` is removed, because the download step always completes the archive set before the install. No item removes a `.deb` between download and install and asserts a fast failure naming the package with no mirror fetch. Removing `--no-download` would bring back the unbounded mirror fetch in the install step, which is the MANDATE defect. Add that probe item, or pin `--no-download` in the wiring contract.
- [should-fix] PR-12 NOT CLOSED. O3 claims that "a typo or `0`" fails before any apt call. Probe item 7 says only "non-positive". A non-numeric value such as `abc` is not covered. In bash that is the classic case where `[ "$x" -le 0 ]` returns 2 and the check is skipped, silently removing the bound. Neither of these has an assertion: an empty value meaning "no bound" (the frontend and backend jobs rely on it), and whether a fractional value is accepted. Add `abc`, `""` and `1.5` cases to item 7.
- PR-9 CLOSED. Probe item 6 uses real sudo and the `sudo timeout --kill-after` form. Dropping `sudo` or `--kill-after` leaves the apt children running or the stall unstopped, so the item goes red.
- PR-10 CLOSED. The release gate requires `rust` and `frontend` to resolve the same key, and probe item 1 covers the key derivation.
- PR-14 CLOSED for the four named wiring checks. See D1 below for what they leave out.
- PR-15 CLOSED. The gate has an exact pass criterion (`cache-hit` true plus `Need to get 0 B` in both jobs) and a named owner. `test.yml` has `workflow_dispatch` (line 9), so the second-run route exists.
- PR-16 CLOSED. The `mutation.yml` backend caller is covered statically by the wiring contract. The catalog-engines Windows/macOS skip is covered by the release gate.

## (2) Defects introduced by the delta

- [should-fix] plan body lines 136-144 (O4 wiring contract) — D1: the wiring contract pins only the callers. It pins nothing inside the new `linux-desktop-deps` composite, and nothing there is guarded by a committed check:
  - the `actions/cache/restore` and `actions/cache/save` steps;
  - save running only on an exact-key miss;
  - the `timeout --kill-after` wrapper;
  - `--no-download`;
  - `shell: bash` on every step;
  - the order resolve → restore → download → prune → save → install.

  Deleting the cache steps, the timeout wrapper, or moving save after install keeps `gates:routing:test` and `gates:contract:check` green. `actionlint` does not read composites (the plan says so). Only the uncommitted probe and the post-push gate would notice. The post-push gate covers the cache half only. Nothing in CI ever exercises the stall bound. This is text pinning in the file's existing `requireText` and `indexOf` style, not the shell extraction machinery that PR-17 rejected, so it does not re-raise PR-17. (confidence: 82)
- [should-fix] plan body lines 148-162 (O4 probe) — D2: probe item 6 simulates only a download stall. O3 adds two further branches that no probe item takes:
  - an update-phase expiry, which prints the label naming the phase "update";
  - the exhausted-deadline branch, where the remaining time is zero or less before the download starts and the step "fails at once".

  Deleting either branch passes all seven items. Add one item for each. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] plan body line 140 (O4) — D3: "Each must go red when its wiring is reverted" names no mechanism. `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1121-1163` holds the revert evidence in its table of mutated strings, for the existing contracts. The plan does not say that rows will be added for the new checks, nor that `readCiWorkflowTexts` (lines 1100-1107, which reads only `test.yml`, `mutation.yml` and the `setup-ci` action) will be extended to read `catalog-engines.yml` and the new composite. The same applies to the check that no workflow other than `release.yml` keeps an inline `apt-get install`. It must scan the whole workflows directory, not a fixed list of three files. Without mutated-string rows, a check that is vacuous (an `includes` that matches the unrelated literal already in the file) would not be detected. (confidence: 80)

## (3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None. The cache half of the central behavior has an anchor that fails on revert (the release gate's `cache-hit` and `Need to get 0 B`, plus probe items 3 and 4). The bound half has probe item 6. Both are uncommitted, which is the accepted PR-17 trade-off.

## LATE OBSERVATIONS

None at or above the reporting threshold.

(No blocker. REVISE because PR-4, PR-11 and PR-12 are corrected obligations that have no assertion failing on revert, and D1 to D3 leave the central cache and bound behavior without a committed regression anchor.)

### lens-tests-r3.txt

Missing context: Bash was denied, and `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` is outside the file-tool sandbox. I judged from the five named rule files and the source and could not read the contract. Global CLAUDE.md rules 4, 6d, 11 and 12b were not readable either. I did not re-raise PR-17 or PR-32.

**(1) Open issues**

- PR-4 CLOSED for restore and save: the `continue-on-error: true` text pin guards the step-level failure. The prune branch is covered by probe item 5, but see finding 1.
- [should-fix] PR-4 NOT CLOSED for the post-download failure paths. See findings 1 and 4.
- PR-11 CLOSED. The step-scoped `--no-download` pin goes red on revert. Probe item 6 has measured evidence (`E: Unable to fetch some archives`, nothing fetched).
- PR-12 CLOSED. Probe item 8 lists `0`, `-1`, `abc`, `1.5` and empty. The rust job's own value is a separate gap (finding 3).
- [should-fix] PR-19 NOT CLOSED. See finding 2.
- PR-25 CLOSED. Probe item 4 (same-size corruption) goes red if the verification step is removed: apt then reports `Need to get 0 B` and dpkg fails, as probe 2 measured.
- [should-fix] PR-26 NOT CLOSED. See finding 5.
- PR-27 CLOSED. Probe item 7 now covers update-phase expiry and the used-up-deadline branch.
- PR-28 CLOSED. There is a rejection-table row per check, and the reader is extended. The step-scoped helper still needs writing; `workflowSteps` (`check-gate-routing.mjs:184`) skips any step with no `run:`, so the restore and save `uses:` steps need a new splitter. That is an executor detail, not a finding.

**(2) Defects introduced by the delta**

1. [should-fix] O2 Fail-open vs probe item 5 — "A failing verification or prune empties the archive directory" is wrong for prune. The order is download → prune → save → install, so a prune failure that empties the directory leaves nothing for the `--no-download` install. That is the "Unable to fetch" failure from probe 2, so a cache optimisation fails the setup step.
   - Probe item 5 asserts exit 0 "after a full download", which only holds if a second download runs. The plan doesn't say one does.
   - A literal implementation fails item 5, and the text gives the executor no correct mechanism. A safe rule would be: on a prune failure, keep the directory and skip the save.
   - Probe 5 injects only a prune failure. The verification-failure and key-derivation-failure branches that Fail-open names have no injected probe, so removing their guards stays green. (confidence: 80)
2. [should-fix] O2 Download set / PR-19 — the resolve-against-the-same-empty-directory fix has no anchor.
   - It isn't in the wiring pins. Every probe item runs in a clean container where the default `/var/cache/apt/archives` is empty, so reverting the resolve to the default directory gives identical results.
   - Add a probe case that seeds the default archive directory with one `.deb` and shows the package is still in the set. Alternatively, add a step-scoped pin that the `--print-uris` command carries the same `Dir::Cache::Archives` option as the download. (confidence: 82)
3. [should-fix] O4 wiring contract, "the `rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes`" — a presence check passes if the value is emptied (`desktop-timeout-minutes: ""`), and empty means unbounded by design (O3 Input). That silently undoes the MANDATE bound, and drifting the value from 18 to anything stays green. Pin the literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"` in the `rust` job and give it a rejection-table row. (confidence: 80)
4. [should-fix] O3 Annotation / probe items 7–8 — the new branch "OOM-killed apt (137 before the deadline) is not labelled" and the "elapsed ≥ bound" condition have no probe case.
   - Item 8's "unknown package" fails in the `--print-uris` resolve, before the update or download commands, so it doesn't exercise a non-timeout exit of those commands.
   - If the label condition were reverted to "any 124 or 137", or "any non-zero", every listed probe item would stay green.
   - Add a case that SIGKILLs apt, or fails the download quickly, before the bound and asserts no `::error::` stall label. (confidence: 85)
5. [should-fix] O4 wiring contract — "the apt network commands use `sudo timeout --kill-after`" is satisfied by one occurrence. If only the download-phase command, the one the MANDATE is about, loses the wrapper while the update keeps it, a substring match stays green. The table's `replaceAll` row only proves the both-removed case. Require one match per network command, or a count of at least 2. (confidence: 80)

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan:** none. The cache hit and the no-mirror-fetch behaviour are anchored by probe item 3 and the CI release gate (`cache-hit` true and `Need to get 0 B` on the next run). The bound is anchored by probe item 7 and the text pin, subject to finding 5.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] The rejection loop accepts any throw (`assert.throws`, `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1158`). A new row can go red because an unrelated contract threw, for example a `TypeError` from a step helper returning `undefined`. This predates the plan. Prefer to assert the specific labelled contract. (confidence: 60)
- [nit] The probe extracts `run:` bodies, but the deadline is carried across steps. Nothing requires the harness to reproduce the real `GITHUB_ENV`/`GITHUB_OUTPUT` hand-off, so a carry bug can only show up in CI. Only the catastrophic form shows there (an immediate fail on the `rust` job); a silently unbounded timeout would not. (confidence: 60)

Both open questions rest on the plan body and its Reviews table. Two named inputs, `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (rules 4, 6d, 11, 12b) and `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md`, were outside the allowed read directories, so I could not read them. I judged against the plan, `gate-tooling.md`, the push skill and the current `check-gate-routing*.mjs`.

**(1) Open issues**

- PR-4 [should-fix] NOT CLOSED, on two points. The plan's own Fail-open invariant is that a failed restore, key derivation, verification, prune or save never fails the step.
  - **Key derivation:** probe item 5 (plan body :206-208) injects an empty key output, which is the result of a failure, not the failure. Item 8 shows that a failing `--print-uris` resolve must fail the step. Key derivation presumably runs in that same step, so the step must tell the two failures apart. If the key command is left under `set -e`, the step goes red on a derivation failure, and the probe still passes because it sets the output empty by hand. The probe needs a derivation failure injected, for example a broken hash command, expecting exit 0 and a full download. (confidence: 82)
  - **`if:` guards:** the committed pins (:180-183) cover only `continue-on-error: true` and save-before-install. The key-non-empty guard on restore and save is not pinned. The container cannot run `uses:` steps, so the probe never exercises these guards. (confidence: 80)
  - **What is closed:** the r3 defect, where probe 5 could not pass. Prune now keeps the files and skips the save, and the third-probe evidence supports that. Injected verification and prune failures are asserted.
- PR-33 CLOSED. Probe item 4 requires the log line, a refetch, and "the run saves". Reverting to "save only on an exact-key miss" turns that assertion red. Item 3 requires "saves nothing" on the second run. The claim that a newer unique key wins the restore is documented `actions/cache` behaviour. It is not probe-testable, and I accept that.
- PR-34 CLOSED. Probe item 1 seeds the default archive directory and requires that package to stay in the set. If the resolve loses `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=`, the set drops it (0 lines versus 1 in the third probe) and the assertion goes red. The same-option pin is a secondary guard.
- PR-35 CLOSED. The literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"` fails on an emptied or changed value.
- PR-36 CLOSED. Probe item 9 covers apt killed with SIGKILL (exit 137 with elapsed under the bound) and a 404 (exit 100). If the label condition reverts to "any non-zero" or "137 regardless of elapsed", both cases print a label and the "no stall label" assertion fails.
- PR-37 CLOSED at plan level. The pin is now one per network command. Its revert proof depends on CR-2, which is carried. The rejection loop uses `replaceAll` and `assert.throws` with no message match (`check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1156-1160`). A row that strips `sudo timeout --kill-after` therefore removes it from both commands and passes on the other command's pin. Each row must assert its own labelled check.
- Not re-judged, as instructed: CR-1, CR-2 and CR-3 (carried), PR-17, PR-32 and PR-46 (skipped), and PR-38 to 41 and PR-44 (arbiter-closed).

**(2) Defects introduced by the DELTA**

- [should-fix] Plan body :126-132 and :206-208 — the new save gate has no anchor on the `uses:` step. The gate is "download fetched at least one file, and prune did not fail". The probe harness can only observe a run-body flag or output, because it cannot evaluate the GitHub `if:` that connects that flag to `actions/cache/save`. Dropping or loosening the save step's `if:`, for example to `always()`, would pass every committed pin and every probe item. It would cache an unpruned set after a prune failure, and save on every run. Only the one-time CI "no save" observation would catch it, and only for the nothing-downloaded case, not the prune-failure case. Pin the save and restore `if:` text, step-scoped, with rejection rows. (confidence: 80)

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None. The bound has probe items 7 to 9 and the committed `sudo timeout --kill-after` and `--no-download` pins. The cache has probe items 1 to 6 and the CI release gate.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

None at 80 confidence or above.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-tests-r5.txt

(1) Open issues

- PR-47 CLOSED. O4 probe item 5 now injects a real key-derivation failure, a broken hash command. It asserts exit 0 and a full download. O2 guards derivation separately from `--print-uris`, so a revert to unguarded `set -e` aborts the step and turns the probe red. Item 8's unknown-package case anchors the other half: a real apt failure still fails the step.
- PR-48 CLOSED at plan altitude. O4's wiring contract now pins the step-scoped `if:` guards for restore and save. It also pins `continue-on-error: true` and the save-before-install order. Each check gets a rejection-table row, and CR-1 and CR-2 carry the step-splitter and specific-label details to diff review. The probe cannot evaluate `if:` expressions, so only this pin and the CI release gate cover that wiring.
- PR-49 **[should-fix] NOT CLOSED**. Probe item 5 asserts a `::warning::` only for the three in-step branches: key derivation, verification and prune. O2 claims one warning for each of five branches, including restore and save. Restore and save are `uses:` steps with `continue-on-error`, so nothing probes or pins their warning. Either name the mechanism and its source (for example, `actions/cache`'s own warning, as a traced premise), or add a wiring-contract pin for any warning step the action adds. If the warning step were deleted, every proposed check would stay green. (See finding below.)
- PR-50 CLOSED. O2 "Save guard state" names the two outputs, `fetched` and `prune ok`, and says a missing value means do not save. Probe item 5 asserts the "do not save" readings on prune failure and key failure. The pinned `if:` anchors the missing-value direction. Diff review should pin the literal `== 'true'` forms, so a loosened `!= 'false'` goes red.

[should-fix] plan O2 "Archive directory" and O4 (wiring contract and probe) — The delta adds a hard requirement: one fixed `/tmp` path, never `mktemp`, because `actions/cache` derives the entry version from the `path` string. No committed or probe check anchors it. The wiring contract doesn't pin that the restore and save `path:` are the same fixed literal. The probe can't catch it either: it runs in a fresh container, so the path is the same whether or not it is fixed, and cache versioning is `actions/cache` behavior. Reverting to a per-run `mktemp -d` would pass `workflows:check`, `gates:routing:test` and the probe. Every restore would then silently miss, which is a quiet return to the uncached 64 MB download in the MANDATE. Only the one-time second-run CI gate would show it. Add a contract row requiring the fixed path literal in both `path:` fields and forbidding `mktemp` for the archive directory. (confidence: 85)

The other delta hunks hold up:
- The restore primary key is now the save key. The first-run gate wording matches it, and the second-run gate still asserts a set-prefix match plus `Need to get 0 B`.
- Probe item 3's fresh-container requirement fixes a self-defeating setup.
- Item 8's message-naming assertion is behavioral.
- CR-4 is correctly carried. Probe item 1 covers only the `MD5Sum:` format, so the `SHA256:` branch is unanchored until diff review.

(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None. Reverting the central behavior breaks a check:

- A key that never matches turns the second-run CI gate red.

They sit outside the file-tool confinement, and a multi-part Bash call was denied. I judged against the plan, the repo rules (`gate-tooling.md`, `async-resource-invariants.md`, push skill 395–420) and the existing contract code at `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:956-1163`. Anything in those unread files is unchecked.

### lens-tests-r6.txt

Status: I read the plan, the delta and the existing routing tests. The sandbox blocked `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` and the `gh api` fetch of `actions/cache` v4, so the findings below rest on the plan's own cited source trace.

**(1) Open issues**

- **[should-fix] PR-49 NOT CLOSED.** The restore half of the fix works, but the save half cannot fire.
  - The plan's own trace says save failures go through `utils.logWarning`, which is `core.info("[warning]…")` (`saveImpl.ts:76`, `actionUtils.ts:28-30`). That path catches the error and does not call `setFailed`, so `steps.<save>.outcome` stays `success`.
  - Yet the new step prints a warning "for each that is `failure`", for restore and for save. The save branch is dead code.
  - Both proofs inject what the real action never produces. O4 pins only that the step "reads both outcomes", as text presence. Probe item 5 runs the step body with the save outcome hand-set to `failure`. A revert of the save-warning logic goes red nowhere, and the real failure stays a plain log line.
  - The restore branch is also narrow. `@actions/cache` downgrades most restore errors to its own `core.warning`, so `setFailed` fires only on rarer paths.
  - To close it, either drop the save outcome from the promise and keep the `[warning]` log text as the stated visibility, or detect save failure another way. The probe should then use a signal the action really emits.

- **PR-59 CLOSED**, with a gap that is not in the pin (see the next section). Pinning the same fixed literal in both `path:` fields and forbidding `mktemp` for that directory would go red on a `mktemp` revert. A new `readCiWorkflowTexts` key and a rejection-table row make that revert visible.

**(2) Defects in the delta or its dependents**

  - O4 pins the resolve and the download to the same `Dir::Cache::Archives`. It pins the cache `path:` fields to each other. It never links the two pairs.
  - If someone edits the apt directory but not the cache path, apt writes `.deb` files where the cache does not look. Restore finds nothing and the save step archives an empty or wrong directory.
  - The job stays green: the install still downloads from the mirror, and fail-open swallows the error. The cache silently stops hitting and the mirror stall returns.
  - Only the one-time probe and the one-time "next run shows `Need to get 0 B`" release gate would notice, and neither recurs.
  - Add a pin that the cache `path:` literal equals the apt archive-directory literal.
- **[nit] O4 pin "outcome-warning step reads both outcomes"** is text presence only. (confidence: 80)
  - An inverted comparison, or a step ordered after a failing install without `if: always()`, still passes it.
  - Only the uncommitted probe would see that. Combined with PR-49 above, this step has no committed assertion on its behavior.

**(3) Blockers elsewhere in the plan**

None. The central behavior has a text-level revert anchor:

- the forwarding under `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`;
- one `sudo timeout --kill-after` per network command;
- `--no-download` on the install;
- the cache steps and their guards.

Each has a rejection-table row. The computed behavior (deadline arithmetic, verification, prune, input validation) is anchored only by the disposable probe. Round 4 already accepted that limitation, and I raise no new premise against it.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- **[should-fix] `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` rejection loop** — the loop uses a bare `assert.throws`. (confidence: 80)

### plan-r1.md

# Plan: Cache and bound the Linux desktop apt install in CI (f-20261106-07)

## Goal

A slow Ubuntu mirror no longer turns the `rust` job red inside a test step. In the normal case,
the WebKitGTK build dependencies come from a GitHub Actions cache instead of the mirror. When the
download does stall, it fails inside the setup step, which matches the push skill's existing
runner-infrastructure rerun exception.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261106-07:

> * **Where:** `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` (uncached `sudo apt-get update` + `apt-get install` of the WebKitGTK desktop build dependencies), `.github/workflows/test.yml:244` (`rust` job `timeout-minutes: 30`, which covers setup and tests together).
> * **Defect:** On the push of 17b782bb (run 42167043743, `rust` job 127725818339) the apt step fetched 63.7 MB in 22 min 52 s (46.4 kB/s) from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` (05:13:03 → 05:36:00Z). Rust setup, clippy and the coverage build then left about 4 minutes for `pnpm test:coverage:backend`, and the job was cancelled at the 30-minute limit in "Run Rust tests with coverage" while tests were still passing. The same job on the two preceding master runs took 8 min 22 s and 8 min 36 s. The pushed diff changed no Rust code. Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download, so a mirror stall becomes a red repository-check step instead of an identified setup failure, and the push skill's setup-step rerun exception does not match it.
> * **Failure scenario:** a mirror slowdown on any push reddens `rust` (or another desktop job) at its timeout inside a test step. `pnpm ci:remote:check` then refuses the next push until someone diagnoses the log and reruns it.
> * **Open question:** cache the desktop apt packages (for example, an apt package cache keyed on the package list), bound the apt step with its own `timeout-minutes` and one retry so a stall fails as a setup step, or give the test step its own budget separate from setup.

## Threat model and non-goals

- External condition: the hosted runner's Ubuntu mirror (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, Ubuntu 26.04
  `resolute`, `amd64v3` pool) serves at 35–55 kB/s for long stretches. Index updates (≈4.6 MB) stay
  fast or at worst ≈30 s; the 63.7 MB package download is the slow part.
- Cache integrity: a restored `.deb` is untrusted until apt has verified it. apt checks every
  cached archive against the size and hash recorded in the signed `Packages` index before it uses
  the file, and downloads the file again when they differ. Branch caches are scoped by GitHub, so
  a feature-branch cache cannot be restored on `master`.
- Non-goal: changing which packages are installed or their versions; changing `release.yml`
  (pinned `ubuntu-24.04` WebKit 2.44 set with a different package list and a 120-minute budget);
  raising any job's `timeout-minutes`.

## Traced premises

- `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` — `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`: `sudo apt-get update`
  then `sudo apt-get install --no-install-recommends -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev
  libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf`. No cache, no bound. Composite steps accept no
  `timeout-minutes`.
- Consumers with `desktop: "true"`: `.github/workflows/test.yml:173` (`frontend`, 90 min) and
  `.github/workflows/test.yml:250` (`rust`, 30 min). `e2e` (test.yml:224) and `frontend-mutation`
  (test.yml:334, mutation.yml:156) call `setup-ci` without `desktop`.
- The same package list is copied verbatim into `.github/workflows/mutation.yml:74-81` (`backend`,
  120 min, scheduled/dispatch only) and `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml:89-97` (Linux leg,
  300 min). Neither uses `setup-ci`.
- Measured step timings on master, 2026-11-06 (`gh api …/runs/<id>/jobs`, job logs in
  `$RUN_TMP/ev/`): `frontend` "Set up repository dependencies" took 20 min 32 s (run 42177925912),
  24 min 14 s (42173575090) and 27 min 59 s (42142451793), against 36–62 s on the others. Every slow
  case is the package download (`Fetched 63.7 MB in 19min 59s (53.1 kB/s)`, `Fetched 49.9 MB in
  23min 11s (35.9 kB/s)`, `Fetched 63.7 MB in 27min 23s (38.7 kB/s)`). `apt-get update` finished in
  0–31 s. On a healthy runner the download is `Fetched 63.7 MB in 4s`.
- `rust` job work after setup: "Run Rust tests with coverage" ends 6–8 min after setup ends, and
  the whole job takes 8–10 min.
- Push skill (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:411-415`): a red job whose failing or timed-out step is
  a setup step (`Set up repository dependencies`, …) gets one `gh run rerun --failed` after the
  run completes. A hang in a later step takes the red-job path.
- `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:276-283` requires actions pinned to a commit SHA only in
  `contents: write` jobs. The jobs touched here are read-only. `pnpm workflows:check` runs
  actionlint 1.7.12 over `.github/workflows/`.
- Repository Actions cache: 11.86 GB active across 26 entries (`gh api …/actions/cache/usage`),
  so GitHub already evicts least-recently-used entries. A key restored on every push stays recent.

## Approach

### O1 — One implementation of the Linux desktop dependency install
A new local composite action, `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`, owns the package
list and the whole install. `setup-ci`'s desktop branch, `mutation.yml` `backend` and the Linux leg
of `catalog-engines.yml` all call it, so the four-way copy of the package list becomes one copy.
This follows from MANDATE "Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download": left alone,
the two copies outside `setup-ci` would still be uncached and unbounded. Package names and
`--no-install-recommends` stay exactly as they are.

### O2 — Downloaded packages come from a cache keyed on exactly what apt would fetch
Order: `apt-get update` → resolve the download set → restore the cache → download only what is
missing → prune → save → install.
- The download set is the output of `apt-get install --print-uris -qq --no-install-recommends
  <packages>` after the update, on the runner as booted (URI, filename, size, SHA256 for each
  missing package). The exact cache key is `linux-desktop-apt-${{ runner.os }}-${ImageOS}-` plus a
  hash of that output. The restore fallback prefix is `linux-desktop-apt-${{ runner.os }}-${ImageOS}-`,
  so after a security update or a runner image bump an older entry still covers most packages.
- The archive directory is a runner-writable directory (not `/var/cache/apt/archives`), handed to
  apt via `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=…`. Download and install both use it, so apt verifies each cached
  file against the index (threat model) and downloads only what is missing or mismatched.
- After the download, `.deb` files whose names are not in the current download set are deleted
  before saving, so carried-forward entries do not grow without bound. The bound is the size of
  one resolved set, ≈64 MB.
- Save happens only when the exact key was not restored, right after the download succeeds and
  before installation, so a long job does not delay it. A failed reservation, because a parallel
  job (`frontend` and `rust`) is saving the same key, is a warning and never fails the job.

### O3 — A stalled download fails as an identified setup step
- Input `timeout-minutes` on `linux-desktop-deps` (default empty = no bound) caps the total network
  time of `apt-get update` plus the package download, both run under `timeout`. The non-network
  `apt-get install` from the local archive runs afterwards and stays unbounded.
- On expiry the step prints a `::error::` annotation naming the mirror stall, the elapsed time and
  the bound, then exits non-zero. The failure is therefore in "Set up repository dependencies"
  (via `setup-ci`), which already matches the push skill's setup-step rerun exception. No skill
  text changes.
- `setup-ci` passes a `desktop-timeout-minutes` input through. Only the `rust` job sets it, to
  **18**. Sizing: 18 min of network time + ≈1 min other setup + ≤10 min observed job work ≤ 29 min,
  under the 30-minute job budget. `frontend` (90 min), `backend` (120) and `catalog-engines` (300)
  absorb the worst observed 28-minute download, so they stay unbounded and are the jobs that
  populate a cold cache on a slow mirror.
- Retry: no in-job retry. A retry would hit the same slow mirror and spend the same budget. The
  push skill's single rerun runs after the whole run completes, so by then `frontend`'s save has
  normally made the rerun a cache hit.

### O4 — Verification
- Static: `pnpm workflows:check` (actionlint and the permission checker) and `pnpm gates:contract:check`
  stay green.
- Disposable local probe, not committed: run the action's shell bodies with a PATH shim for
  `apt-get`/`sudo`. It must show (a) a shim that sleeps past a 0.05-minute bound makes the step
  exit non-zero with the `::error::` line, (b) the key derived from canned `--print-uris` output is
  stable across reorderings and changes when one SHA256 changes, and (c) the prune deletes a stale
  `.deb` and keeps the current ones.
- CI, after the push: the first `Test` run on the pushed SHA shows the `rust` and `frontend` logs
  resolving a key and one of them saving it. A second run (the next push, or `gh workflow run
  test.yml --ref master -R felixabeck/en-croissant`) shows an exact-key restore and `Need to get
  0 B` (or only a small delta) in the download step for both desktop jobs.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (technical, under `full auto`):
- **Question:** cache, bound with retry, or separate test budget? **Chosen:** cache (O2), plus a
  per-job network bound only where the job budget cannot absorb a stall (O3). **Rejected:**
  bound-and-retry alone, because the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled master runs, so retries
  would turn red often and still download 64 MB at ≈40 kB/s. A separate test-step budget or a
  larger job timeout leaves the 20–28 minute download in every slow run and still fails in a test
  step. Swapping mirrors is ruled out because nothing in our evidence shows which mirror is
  healthy, and a hard-coded host would be another observation-pinned URL. A third-party
  apt-cache action is ruled out because `actions/cache` restore/save covers it with an action this
  repository already uses (`mutation.yml`). **Reason:** the measurements in Traced premises.
  **Reversal path:** delete `linux-desktop-deps`'s cache steps; the install falls back to the
  mirror.
- **Question:** unify the copies in `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml`? **Chosen:** yes, O1.
  **Rejected:** caching only `setup-ci`, which leaves two uncached copies of the same list (rule
  11). `release.yml` stays separate because its list and pins differ.

## Risks / open questions

- On a cold cache with a slow mirror, `rust` can still fail at its 18-minute bound. That is the
  intended identified setup failure; the rerun normally hits the cache saved by `frontend`.
- GitHub cache LRU eviction above 10 GB could evict the key during a quiet week. The cost is one
  cold download, so it is a known limit.

## Not part of this task

- `release.yml`'s pinned apt install.
- f-20261106-02 (macOS hosted-runner loss; co-located, not planned in this plan-only run).
- Any change to job `timeout-minutes` or to the push skill's rerun rule.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — `linux-desktop-deps` action and its three callers
Files: `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml` (new), `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`,
`.github/workflows/test.yml` (`rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`),
`.github/workflows/mutation.yml` (`backend`), `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml` (Linux leg).
No auth, persistence, concurrency or API surface. Role: normal.
PROOF COMMAND: `pnpm workflows:check && pnpm gates:contract:check`, then the O4 disposable probe,
reproducing its output. CI evidence per O4 is collected at release.

### plan-r2.md

# Plan: Cache and bound the Linux desktop apt install in CI (f-20261106-07)

## Goal

A slow Ubuntu mirror no longer turns the `rust` job red inside a test step. In the normal case,
the WebKitGTK build dependencies come from a GitHub Actions cache instead of the mirror. When the
download does stall, it fails inside the setup step, which matches the push skill's existing
runner-infrastructure rerun exception.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261106-07:

> * **Where:** `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` (uncached `sudo apt-get update` + `apt-get install` of the WebKitGTK desktop build dependencies), `.github/workflows/test.yml:244` (`rust` job `timeout-minutes: 30`, which covers setup and tests together).
> * **Defect:** On the push of 17b782bb (run 42167043743, `rust` job 127725818339) the apt step fetched 63.7 MB in 22 min 52 s (46.4 kB/s) from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` (05:13:03 → 05:36:00Z). Rust setup, clippy and the coverage build then left about 4 minutes for `pnpm test:coverage:backend`, and the job was cancelled at the 30-minute limit in "Run Rust tests with coverage" while tests were still passing. The same job on the two preceding master runs took 8 min 22 s and 8 min 36 s. The pushed diff changed no Rust code. Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download, so a mirror stall becomes a red repository-check step instead of an identified setup failure, and the push skill's setup-step rerun exception does not match it.
> * **Failure scenario:** a mirror slowdown on any push reddens `rust` (or another desktop job) at its timeout inside a test step. `pnpm ci:remote:check` then refuses the next push until someone diagnoses the log and reruns it.
> * **Open question:** cache the desktop apt packages (for example, an apt package cache keyed on the package list), bound the apt step with its own `timeout-minutes` and one retry so a stall fails as a setup step, or give the test step its own budget separate from setup.

## Threat model and non-goals

- External condition: the hosted runner's Ubuntu mirror (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, Ubuntu 26.04
  `resolute`, `amd64v3` pool) serves at 35–55 kB/s for long stretches. Index updates (≈4.6 MB) stay
  fast or at worst ≈30 s; the 63.7 MB package download is the slow part.
- Cache integrity: a restored `.deb` is untrusted until apt has verified it. apt checks every
  cached archive against the size and hash recorded in the signed `Packages` index before it uses
  the file, and downloads the file again when they differ. Branch caches are scoped by GitHub, so
  a feature-branch cache cannot be restored on `master`.
- Non-goal: changing which packages are installed or their versions; changing `release.yml`
  (pinned `ubuntu-24.04` WebKit 2.44 set with a different package list and a 120-minute budget);
  raising any job's `timeout-minutes`.

## Traced premises

- `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` — `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`: `sudo apt-get update`
  then `sudo apt-get install --no-install-recommends -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev
  libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf`. No cache, no bound. Composite steps accept no
  `timeout-minutes`.
- Consumers with `desktop: "true"`: `.github/workflows/test.yml:173` (`frontend`, 90 min) and
  `.github/workflows/test.yml:250` (`rust`, 30 min). `e2e` (test.yml:224) and `frontend-mutation`
  (test.yml:334, mutation.yml:156) call `setup-ci` without `desktop`.
- The same package list is copied verbatim into `.github/workflows/mutation.yml:74-81` (`backend`,
  120 min, scheduled/dispatch only) and `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml:89-97` (Linux leg,
  300 min). Neither uses `setup-ci`.
- Measured step timings on master, 2026-11-06 (`gh api …/runs/<id>/jobs`, job logs in
  `$RUN_TMP/ev/`): `frontend` "Set up repository dependencies" took 20 min 32 s (run 42177925912),
  24 min 14 s (42173575090) and 27 min 59 s (42142451793), against 36–62 s on the others. Every slow
  case is the package download (`Fetched 63.7 MB in 19min 59s (53.1 kB/s)`, `Fetched 49.9 MB in
  23min 11s (35.9 kB/s)`, `Fetched 63.7 MB in 27min 23s (38.7 kB/s)`). `apt-get update` finished in
  0–31 s. On a healthy runner the download is `Fetched 63.7 MB in 4s`.
- `rust` job work after setup: "Run Rust tests with coverage" ends 6–8 min after setup ends, and
  the whole job takes 8–10 min.
- Push skill (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:411-415`): a red job whose failing or timed-out step is
  a setup step (`Set up repository dependencies`, …) gets one `gh run rerun --failed` after the
  run completes. A hang in a later step takes the red-job path.
- `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:276-283` requires actions pinned to a commit SHA only in
  `contents: write` jobs. The jobs touched here are read-only. `pnpm workflows:check` runs
  actionlint 1.7.12 over `.github/workflows/`.
- `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1075-1096` (`ciWorkflowContracts`, run by `gates:routing:test`)
  pins the `setup-ci` composite text, including the literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`.
- Probe 2026-11-06 in a disposable `ubuntu:24.04` container with real apt: `--print-uris` prints
  `'URI' filename size MD5Sum:…`. `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=<dir> --download-only` stores the `.deb`
  there, root-owned 0644. After the file was overwritten with garbage, apt fetched it again
  (`Need to get 106 kB`). With an intact file, apt reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB of archives`.
- Repository Actions cache: 11.86 GB active across 26 entries (`gh api …/actions/cache/usage`),
  so GitHub already evicts least-recently-used entries. A key restored on every push stays recent.

## Approach

### O1 — One implementation of the Linux desktop dependency install
A new local composite action, `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`, owns the package
list and the whole install. `setup-ci`'s desktop branch, `mutation.yml` `backend` and the Linux leg
of `catalog-engines.yml` all call it, so the four-way copy of the package list becomes one copy.
This follows from MANDATE "Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download": left alone,
the two copies outside `setup-ci` would still be uncached and unbounded. Package names and
`--no-install-recommends` stay exactly as they are. The `setup-ci` step that calls the action keeps
the literal condition `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`, which `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`
pins. The `catalog-engines.yml` call keeps `if: runner.os == 'Linux'`. Every `run:` step in the
composite declares `shell: bash`, because composite steps do not inherit a workflow's
`defaults.run.shell`.

### O2 — Downloaded packages come from a cache keyed on exactly what apt would fetch
Order: `apt-get update` → resolve the download set → restore the cache → download only what is
missing → prune → save → install.
- **Download set.** After the update, `apt-get install --print-uris -qq --no-install-recommends
  <packages>` on the runner as booted prints one `'URI' filename size checksum` line per missing
  package. The resolve step emits the exact key and the fallback prefix as step outputs, because
  `actions/cache` takes its key as an expression and runner environment variables are not in that
  context. The key is `linux-desktop-apt-<VERSION_CODENAME>-<dpkg architecture>-` plus a hash of
  the sorted `filename size checksum` columns. The mirror URI is excluded, so a different mirror
  host yields the same key for the same packages. The prefix alone is the restore fallback, so
  after a security update or a runner image bump an older entry still covers most packages.
- **Archive directory.** A world-traversable directory under `/tmp`, created by the runner user,
  is handed to apt as its archive directory for both the download and the install. apt verifies
  every cached file against the signed index and fetches it again on mismatch (threat model).
  Only what is missing or mismatched is downloaded.
- **What the cache holds.** The cache `path` is the `.deb` files only, never the directory. apt
  run as root leaves a root-owned `lock` and an `_apt`-owned 0700 `partial/` there, which
  `actions/cache/save` running as the runner user cannot read.
- **Prune.** After the download, `.deb` files whose names are not in the current download set are
  deleted before saving, so carried-forward entries do not grow. The bound is one resolved set,
  ≈64 MB.
- **Save.** Only when the exact key was not restored, right after the download succeeds and before
  installation. A failed save, including a reservation conflict with a parallel job (`frontend`
  and `rust`), is a warning and never fails the job.
- **Fail-open.** The cache is an optimisation. A failed restore, key derivation, prune or save
  degrades to the plain bounded mirror download of O3 and never fails the step. A non-zero exit
  from the `--print-uris` resolve is an apt failure, and the step fails with apt's own error, not
  a stall label. An empty download set with exit 0 means everything is already installed: no cache
  steps run.

### O3 — A stalled download fails as an identified setup step
- **Input.** `linux-desktop-deps` takes `timeout-minutes`. Empty means no bound. Any other value
  that is not a positive number fails the step before any apt call, so a typo or `0` cannot
  silently remove the bound.
- **Shared deadline.** One deadline covers the network time of `apt-get update` plus the package
  download. Each command runs as `sudo timeout --kill-after=<grace> <remaining> apt-get …`, so the
  signal is sent as root and reaches apt and its `_apt` method children. If the remaining time
  reaches zero or less before the download starts, the step fails at once.
- **Install.** The install from the local archive runs with `--no-download`. A missing archive
  then fails fast and by name instead of starting an unbounded mirror fetch.
- **Annotation.** Only a `timeout` expiry (exit 124, or 137 after kill) prints the `::error::`
  annotation. It names the mirror stall, the phase (update or download), the elapsed time and the
  bound. Any other apt failure keeps apt's own exit status and output. Either way the step fails
  inside "Set up repository dependencies" (via `setup-ci`), which already matches the push
  skill's setup-step rerun exception. No skill text changes.
- **Who is bounded.** `setup-ci` passes a `desktop-timeout-minutes` input through. Only the `rust`
  job sets it, to **18**. Sizing: 18 min of network time + ≈1 min other setup + ≤10 min observed
  job work ≤ 29 min, under the 30-minute job budget. `frontend` (90 min), `backend` (120) and
  `catalog-engines` (300) absorb the worst observed 28-minute download, so they stay unbounded.
  They are the jobs that populate a cold cache on a slow mirror.
- **Retry.** No in-job retry. A retry would hit the same slow mirror and spend the same budget. The
  push skill's single rerun runs after the whole run completes, so by then `frontend`'s save has
  normally made the rerun a cache hit.

### O4 — Verification
- **Wiring contract (committed).** Extend the existing CI wiring contracts in
  `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (run by `gates:routing:test` inside
  `gates:contract:check`), in its `requireText`/`forbid` style. Checks:
  - the `rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes`;
  - `setup-ci` forwards that input to `linux-desktop-deps` under `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`;
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` and `catalog-engines.yml` call `linux-desktop-deps`;
  - no workflow except `release.yml` keeps an inline `apt-get install` of `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev`.

  Each must go red when its wiring is reverted.
- **Static.** `pnpm workflows:check` (actionlint and the permission checker) covers
  `.github/workflows/` only, not composite actions. The composite's `run:` bodies are additionally
  checked with `shellcheck`.
- **Real-apt probe (disposable, not committed).** Run the composite's actual `run:` bodies,
  extracted from `action.yml`, as a non-root user with real `sudo` in a disposable `ubuntu:26.04`
  container. It must show:
  1. the real `--print-uris` format parses into a non-empty download set, and the key does not
     change when only the mirror host differs;
  2. a cold run downloads, and the saved `.deb` set (the cache `path`) is readable by the
     non-root user;
  3. a second run with that set restored reports `Need to get 0 B`;
  4. a corrupted cached `.deb` is fetched again;
  5. the prune removes a stale `.deb`;
  6. a stalled download is stopped at the bound by the `sudo timeout` form with the `::error::`
     line. Simulate the stall by pointing apt at a proxy that accepts connections and never
     answers.
  7. a non-positive `timeout-minutes` fails before any apt call, and a non-timeout apt failure
     (an unknown package) carries no stall label.
- **CI, release gate.**
  - The first `Test` run on the pushed SHA must show the `rust` and `frontend` logs resolving the
    same key and one of them saving it.
  - The next `Test` run (the next push, or `gh workflow run test.yml --ref master -R
    felixabeck/en-croissant`) must show `cache-hit` true on the exact key and `Need to get 0 B` in
    both desktop jobs' download step. The orchestrator confirms this before closing the finding.
  - The push also triggers `catalog-engines.yml` (its own path filter): the Linux leg must run the
    action, and the Windows and macOS legs must skip it and stay green.
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` runs only on schedule or dispatch. Its call is proven statically by the
    wiring contract, and functionally by the same action in `rust` and `frontend`. The Sunday
    2026-11-08 run is a follow-up observation, not a closure gate. A dispatch would start the full
    mutation matrix.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (technical, under `full auto`):
- **Question:** cache, bound with retry, or separate test budget? **Chosen:** cache (O2), plus a
  per-job network bound only where the job budget cannot absorb a stall (O3). **Rejected:**
  bound-and-retry alone, because the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled master runs, so retries
  would turn red often and still download 64 MB at ≈40 kB/s. A separate test-step budget or a
  larger job timeout leaves the 20–28 minute download in every slow run and still fails in a test
  step. Swapping mirrors is ruled out because nothing in our evidence shows which mirror is
  healthy, and a hard-coded host would be another observation-pinned URL. A third-party
  apt-cache action is ruled out because `actions/cache` restore/save covers it with an action this
  repository already uses (`mutation.yml`). **Reason:** the measurements in Traced premises.
  **Reversal path:** delete `linux-desktop-deps`'s cache steps; the install falls back to the
  mirror.
- **Question:** unify the copies in `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml`? **Chosen:** yes, O1.
  **Rejected:** caching only `setup-ci`, which leaves two uncached copies of the same list (rule
  11). `release.yml` stays separate because its list and pins differ.

## Risks / open questions

- On a cold cache with a slow mirror, `rust` can still fail at its 18-minute bound. That is the
  intended identified setup failure; the rerun normally hits the cache saved by `frontend`.
- GitHub cache LRU eviction above 10 GB could evict the key during a quiet week. The cost is one
  cold download, so it is a known limit.
- A push that cold-builds Rust (new `Cargo.lock`) and coincides with a download just under 18
  minutes can still exceed the 30-minute job budget in a later step. That needs both a cold Rust
  cache and a cold apt cache on a slow mirror. It is a known limit, not covered by this change.

## Not part of this task

- `release.yml`'s pinned apt install.
- f-20261106-02 (macOS hosted-runner loss; co-located, not planned in this plan-only run).
- Any change to job `timeout-minutes` or to the push skill's rerun rule.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — `linux-desktop-deps` action and its three callers
Files: `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml` (new), `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`,
`.github/workflows/test.yml` (`rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`),
`.github/workflows/mutation.yml` (`backend`), `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml` (Linux leg).
No auth, persistence, concurrency or API surface. Role: normal.
Also `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (O4 wiring contract).
PROOF COMMAND: `pnpm workflows:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm gates:contract:check`,
`shellcheck` over the composite's extracted `run:` bodies, then the O4 real-apt container probe,
reproducing its output. The O4 CI release gate is collected after the push and before the finding
closes.

review-error-handling APPROVED (with should-fix findings) · review-tests REVISE · review-platform-semantics REVISE ·

review-persisted-state, review-pgn-index NOT APPLICABLE + APPROVED (reasons checked: CI YAML only, no class surface).

### plan-r3.md

# Plan: Cache and bound the Linux desktop apt install in CI (f-20261106-07)

## Goal

A slow Ubuntu mirror no longer turns the `rust` job red inside a test step. In the normal case,
the WebKitGTK build dependencies come from a GitHub Actions cache instead of the mirror. When the
download does stall, it fails inside the setup step, which matches the push skill's existing
runner-infrastructure rerun exception.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261106-07:

> * **Where:** `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` (uncached `sudo apt-get update` + `apt-get install` of the WebKitGTK desktop build dependencies), `.github/workflows/test.yml:244` (`rust` job `timeout-minutes: 30`, which covers setup and tests together).
> * **Defect:** On the push of 17b782bb (run 42167043743, `rust` job 127725818339) the apt step fetched 63.7 MB in 22 min 52 s (46.4 kB/s) from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` (05:13:03 → 05:36:00Z). Rust setup, clippy and the coverage build then left about 4 minutes for `pnpm test:coverage:backend`, and the job was cancelled at the 30-minute limit in "Run Rust tests with coverage" while tests were still passing. The same job on the two preceding master runs took 8 min 22 s and 8 min 36 s. The pushed diff changed no Rust code. Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download, so a mirror stall becomes a red repository-check step instead of an identified setup failure, and the push skill's setup-step rerun exception does not match it.
> * **Failure scenario:** a mirror slowdown on any push reddens `rust` (or another desktop job) at its timeout inside a test step. `pnpm ci:remote:check` then refuses the next push until someone diagnoses the log and reruns it.
> * **Open question:** cache the desktop apt packages (for example, an apt package cache keyed on the package list), bound the apt step with its own `timeout-minutes` and one retry so a stall fails as a setup step, or give the test step its own budget separate from setup.

## Threat model and non-goals

- External condition: the hosted runner's Ubuntu mirror (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, Ubuntu 26.04
  `resolute`, `amd64v3` pool) serves at 35–55 kB/s for long stretches. Index updates (≈4.6 MB) stay
  fast or at worst ≈30 s; the 63.7 MB package download is the slow part.
- Cache integrity: a restored `.deb` is untrusted until it has been checked against the
  resolved download set. apt 3.2 checks only the size of an already-present archive. A
  same-size corrupted file passes the download stage (`Need to get 0 B`) and fails only later in
  dpkg (probe in Traced premises). The action therefore checks each restored file itself (O2).
  Branch caches are scoped by GitHub, so a feature-branch cache cannot be restored on `master`.
  Anyone who can write `master`'s cache can already change the workflow, so deliberate poisoning
  is out of scope. Only corruption is in scope.
- Non-goal: changing which packages are installed or their versions; changing `release.yml`
  (pinned `ubuntu-24.04` WebKit 2.44 set with a different package list and a 120-minute budget);
  raising any job's `timeout-minutes`.

## Traced premises

- `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` — `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`: `sudo apt-get update`
  then `sudo apt-get install --no-install-recommends -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev
  libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf`. No cache, no bound. Composite steps accept no
  `timeout-minutes`.
- Consumers with `desktop: "true"`: `.github/workflows/test.yml:173` (`frontend`, 90 min) and
  `.github/workflows/test.yml:250` (`rust`, 30 min). `e2e` (test.yml:224) and `frontend-mutation`

  120 min, scheduled/dispatch only) and `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml:89-97` (Linux leg,
  300 min). Neither uses `setup-ci`.
- Measured step timings on master, 2026-11-06 (`gh api …/runs/<id>/jobs`, job logs in
  `$RUN_TMP/ev/`): `frontend` "Set up repository dependencies" took 20 min 32 s (run 42177925912),
  24 min 14 s (42173575090) and 27 min 59 s (42142451793), against 36–62 s on the others. Every slow
  case is the package download (`Fetched 63.7 MB in 19min 59s (53.1 kB/s)`, `Fetched 49.9 MB in

- `rust` job work after setup: "Run Rust tests with coverage" ends 6–8 min after setup ends, and

- Push skill (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:411-415`): a red job whose failing or timed-out step is

- `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:276-283` requires actions pinned to a commit SHA only in
