- Probe 2026-11-06 in a disposable `ubuntu:24.04` container with real apt: `--print-uris` prints
  `'URI' filename size MD5Sum:…`. `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=<dir> --download-only` stores the `.deb`
  there, root-owned 0644. After the file was overwritten with garbage, apt fetched it again
  (`Need to get 106 kB`). With an intact file, apt reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB of archives`.
- Second probe, 2026-11-06, `ubuntu:26.04` (apt 3.2.0): after four bytes were overwritten in a
  cached `.deb` without changing its size, `--download-only` reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB`,
  so the file was not re-verified. `--no-download` install then failed in dpkg
  (`Sub-process /usr/bin/dpkg returned an error code (1)`). With the `.deb` removed, the
  `--no-download` install failed with `E: Unable to fetch some archives` and fetched nothing. In
  the archive directory, apt left `lock` as root 0640 and `partial/` as `_apt` 0700.
- Repository Actions cache: 11.86 GB active across 26 entries (`gh api …/actions/cache/usage`),
  so GitHub already evicts least-recently-used entries. A key restored on every push stays recent.

## Approach

### O1 — One implementation of the Linux desktop dependency install
A new local composite action, `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`, owns the package
list and the whole install. `setup-ci`'s desktop branch, `mutation.yml` `backend` and the Linux leg
of `catalog-engines.yml` all call it, so the four-way copy of the package list becomes one copy.
This follows from MANDATE "Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download": left alone,
the two copies outside `setup-ci` would still be uncached and unbounded. Package names and
`--no-install-recommends` stay exactly as they are. The `setup-ci` step that calls the action keeps
the literal condition `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`, which `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`
pins. The `catalog-engines.yml` call keeps `if: runner.os == 'Linux'`. Every `run:` step in the
composite declares `shell: bash`, because composite steps do not inherit a workflow's
`defaults.run.shell`.

### O2 — Downloaded packages come from a cache keyed on exactly what apt would fetch
Order: `apt-get update` → resolve the download set → restore the cache → download only what is
missing → prune → save → install.
- **Download set.** After the update, `apt-get install --print-uris -qq --no-install-recommends
  <packages>` on the runner as booted prints one `'URI' filename size checksum` line per missing
  package. The resolve runs against the same, still empty, archive directory, so the set never
  depends on the runner's default `/var/cache/apt/archives`. The resolve step emits the exact key and the fallback prefix as step outputs, because
  `actions/cache` takes its key as an expression and runner environment variables are not in that
  context. The key is `linux-desktop-apt-<VERSION_CODENAME>-<dpkg architecture>-` plus a hash of
  the sorted `filename size checksum` columns. The mirror URI is excluded, so a different mirror
  host yields the same key for the same packages. The prefix alone is the restore fallback, so
  after a security update or a runner image bump an older entry still covers most packages.
- **Archive directory.** A world-traversable directory under `/tmp`, created by the runner user,
  is handed to apt as its archive directory for both the download and the install. apt verifies
  every cached file against the signed index and fetches it again on mismatch (threat model).
  Only what is missing or mismatched is downloaded.
- **What the cache holds.** The cache `path` is the `.deb` files only, never the directory. apt
  run as root leaves a root-owned `lock` and an `_apt`-owned 0700 `partial/` there, which
  `actions/cache/save` running as the runner user cannot read.
- **Verify restored files.** Right after the restore, every restored `.deb` whose size or
  checksum differs from its line in the download set is deleted, so the download fetches it
  again. apt alone would accept a same-size corrupted file (threat model).
- **Prune.** After the download, `.deb` files whose names are not in the current download set are
  deleted before saving, so carried-forward entries do not grow. The bound is one resolved set,
  ≈64 MB.
- **Save.** Only when the exact key was not restored, right after the download succeeds and before
  installation. A failed save, including a reservation conflict with a parallel job (`frontend`
  and `rust`), is a warning and never fails the job.
- **Fail-open.** The cache is an optimisation. A failed restore, key derivation, verification,
  prune or save degrades to the plain bounded mirror download of O3 and never fails the step.
  Mechanism: the restore and save steps run only when the key output is non-empty and carry
  `continue-on-error: true`. A failing verification or prune empties the archive directory,
  so everything is downloaded, and the step continues. A non-zero exit
  from the `--print-uris` resolve is an apt failure, and the step fails with apt's own error, not
  a stall label. An empty download set with exit 0 means everything is already installed: no cache
  steps run.

### O3 — A stalled download fails as an identified setup step
- **Input.** `linux-desktop-deps` takes `timeout-minutes`. Empty means no bound. Any other value
  that is not a positive number fails the step before any apt call, so a typo or `0` cannot
  silently remove the bound.
- **Shared deadline.** One wall-clock deadline, starting when `apt-get update` starts and carried
  across steps, covers the update, the cache restore and verification, and the package download. Each command runs as `sudo timeout --kill-after=<grace> <remaining> apt-get …`, so the
  signal is sent as root and reaches apt and its `_apt` method children. If the remaining time
  reaches zero or less before the download starts, the step fails at once.
- **Install.** The install from the local archive runs with `--no-download`. A missing archive
  then fails fast and by name instead of starting an unbounded mirror fetch.
- **Annotation.** Only a deadline expiry prints the `::error::` annotation: a `timeout` exit (124,
  or 137 after kill) with elapsed time at or past the bound, or remaining time at zero before the
  download starts. An OOM-killed apt (137 before the deadline) is not labelled. It names the mirror stall, the phase (update or download), the elapsed time and the
  bound. Any other apt failure keeps apt's own exit status and output. Either way the step fails
  inside "Set up repository dependencies" (via `setup-ci`), which already matches the push
  skill's setup-step rerun exception. No skill text changes.
- **Who is bounded.** `setup-ci` passes a `desktop-timeout-minutes` input through. Only the `rust`
  job sets it, to **18**. Sizing: 18 min of network time + ≈1 min other setup + ≤10 min observed
  job work ≤ 29 min, under the 30-minute job budget. `frontend` (90 min), `backend` (120) and
  `catalog-engines` (300) absorb the worst observed 28-minute download, so they stay unbounded.
  They are the jobs that populate a cold cache on a slow mirror.
- **Retry.** No in-job retry. A retry would hit the same slow mirror and spend the same budget. The
  push skill's single rerun runs after the whole run completes, so by then `frontend`'s save has
  normally made the rerun a cache hit.

### O4 — Verification
- **Wiring contract (committed).** Extend the existing CI wiring contracts in
  `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (run by `gates:routing:test` inside
  `gates:contract:check`), in its `requireText`/`forbid` style. Checks:
  - the `rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes`;
  - `setup-ci` forwards that input to `linux-desktop-deps` under `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`;
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` and `catalog-engines.yml` call `linux-desktop-deps`;
  - `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` keep no inline `apt-get install` of
    `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev`;
  - inside `linux-desktop-deps`: the restore and save steps exist with `continue-on-error: true`,
    the save step comes before the install step, the apt network commands use
    `sudo timeout --kill-after`, and the install uses `--no-download`.

  Each check gets a row in the existing mutated-string rejection table
  (`check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`, the loop after `ciWorkflowContracts`), so it is shown to go red
  when its wiring is reverted. Checks scoped to a step match that step's text, not the whole
  file. `readCiWorkflowTexts` is extended to read `catalog-engines.yml` and the new action.
- **Static.** `pnpm workflows:check` (actionlint and the permission checker) covers
  `.github/workflows/` only, not composite actions. The composite's `run:` bodies are additionally
  checked with `shellcheck`.
- **Real-apt probe (disposable, not committed).** Run the composite's actual `run:` bodies,
  extracted from `action.yml`, as a non-root user with real `sudo` in a disposable `ubuntu:26.04`
  container. Record `sudo --version` and `apt --version`. Pass any proxy to apt through `-o`
  options, because `sudo` drops `http_proxy`. It must show:
  1. the real `--print-uris` format parses into a non-empty download set, and the key does not
     change when only the mirror host differs;
  2. a cold run downloads, and the saved `.deb` set (the cache `path`) is readable by the
     non-root user;
  3. a second run with that set restored reports `Need to get 0 B`;
  4. a same-size corrupted restored `.deb` is deleted by the verification and fetched again;
  5. the prune removes a stale `.deb`, and an injected prune failure still ends the step with
     exit 0 after a full download;
  6. a `.deb` removed between download and install makes the `--no-download` install fail at once
     without a mirror fetch;
  7. stalls are stopped by the `sudo timeout` form with the labelled `::error::` line, in both
     the update phase and the download phase. A deadline already used up before the download
     fails at once with the label. Simulate the stall with a proxy that accepts connections and
     never answers, and keep the probe bound below apt's own 120-second idle timeout;
  8. `timeout-minutes` values `0`, `-1` and `abc` fail before any apt call, `1.5` is accepted, an
     empty value runs unbounded, and a non-timeout apt failure (an unknown package) carries no
     stall label.
- **CI, release gate.**
  - The first `Test` run on the pushed SHA must show the `rust` and `frontend` logs resolving the
    same key and one of them saving it.
  - The next `Test` run (the next push, or `gh workflow run test.yml --ref master -R
    felixabeck/chessriddle-studio`) must show `cache-hit` true on the exact key and `Need to get 0 B` in
    both desktop jobs' download step. The orchestrator confirms this before closing the finding.
    If the two jobs resolve different keys, rerun once before suspecting the wiring, because apt
    picks its `amd64v3` variant pool from the host CPU.
  - The push also triggers `catalog-engines.yml` (its own path filter): the Linux leg must run the
    action, and the Windows and macOS legs must skip it and stay green.
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` runs only on schedule or dispatch. Its call is proven statically by the
    wiring contract, and functionally by the same action in `rust` and `frontend`. The Sunday
    2026-11-08 run is a follow-up observation, not a closure gate. A dispatch would start the full
    mutation matrix.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (technical, under `full auto`):
- **Question:** cache, bound with retry, or separate test budget? **Chosen:** cache (O2), plus a
  per-job network bound only where the job budget cannot absorb a stall (O3). **Rejected:**
  bound-and-retry alone, because the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled master runs, so retries
  would turn red often and still download 64 MB at ≈40 kB/s. A separate test-step budget or a
  larger job timeout leaves the 20–28 minute download in every slow run and still fails in a test
  step. Swapping mirrors is ruled out because nothing in our evidence shows which mirror is
  healthy, and a hard-coded host would be another observation-pinned URL. A third-party
  apt-cache action is ruled out because `actions/cache` restore/save covers it with an action this
  repository already uses (`mutation.yml`). **Reason:** the measurements in Traced premises.
  **Reversal path:** delete `linux-desktop-deps`'s cache steps; the install falls back to the
  mirror.
- **Question:** unify the copies in `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml`? **Chosen:** yes, O1.
  **Rejected:** caching only `setup-ci`, which leaves two uncached copies of the same list (rule
  11). `release.yml` stays separate because its list and pins differ.

## Risks / open questions

- On a cold cache with a slow mirror, `rust` can still fail at its 18-minute bound. That is the
  intended identified setup failure; the rerun normally hits the cache saved by `frontend`.
- GitHub cache LRU eviction above 10 GB could evict the key during a quiet week. The cost is one
  cold download, so it is a known limit.
- A push that cold-builds Rust (new `Cargo.lock`) and coincides with a download just under 18
  minutes can still exceed the 30-minute job budget in a later step. That needs both a cold Rust
  cache and a cold apt cache on a slow mirror. It is a known limit, not covered by this change.
- `frontend` stays unbounded. A download longer than its remaining budget (more than about 75
  minutes) would still redden it in a later step. The worst observed download is 28 minutes.

## Not part of this task

- `release.yml`'s pinned apt install.
- f-20261106-02 (macOS hosted-runner loss; co-located, not planned in this plan-only run).
- Any change to job `timeout-minutes` or to the push skill's rerun rule.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — `linux-desktop-deps` action and its three callers
Files: `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml` (new), `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`,
`.github/workflows/test.yml` (`rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`),
`.github/workflows/mutation.yml` (`backend`), `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml` (Linux leg).
No auth, persistence, concurrency or API surface. Role: normal.
Also `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (O4 wiring contract).
PROOF COMMAND: `pnpm workflows:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm gates:contract:check`,
`shellcheck` over the composite's extracted `run:` bodies, then the O4 real-apt container probe,
reproducing its output. The O4 CI release gate is collected after the push and before the finding
closes.

### plan-r4.md

# Plan: Cache and bound the Linux desktop apt install in CI (f-20261106-07)

## Goal

A slow Ubuntu mirror no longer turns the `rust` job red inside a test step. In the normal case,
the WebKitGTK build dependencies come from a GitHub Actions cache instead of the mirror. When the
download does stall, it fails inside the setup step, which matches the push skill's existing
runner-infrastructure rerun exception.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261106-07:

> * **Where:** `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` (uncached `sudo apt-get update` + `apt-get install` of the WebKitGTK desktop build dependencies), `.github/workflows/test.yml:244` (`rust` job `timeout-minutes: 30`, which covers setup and tests together).
> * **Defect:** On the push of 17b782bb (run 42167043743, `rust` job 127725818339) the apt step fetched 63.7 MB in 22 min 52 s (46.4 kB/s) from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` (05:13:03 → 05:36:00Z). Rust setup, clippy and the coverage build then left about 4 minutes for `pnpm test:coverage:backend`, and the job was cancelled at the 30-minute limit in "Run Rust tests with coverage" while tests were still passing. The same job on the two preceding master runs took 8 min 22 s and 8 min 36 s. The pushed diff changed no Rust code. Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download, so a mirror stall becomes a red repository-check step instead of an identified setup failure, and the push skill's setup-step rerun exception does not match it.
> * **Failure scenario:** a mirror slowdown on any push reddens `rust` (or another desktop job) at its timeout inside a test step. `pnpm ci:remote:check` then refuses the next push until someone diagnoses the log and reruns it.
> * **Open question:** cache the desktop apt packages (for example, an apt package cache keyed on the package list), bound the apt step with its own `timeout-minutes` and one retry so a stall fails as a setup step, or give the test step its own budget separate from setup.

## Threat model and non-goals

- External condition: the hosted runner's Ubuntu mirror (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, Ubuntu 26.04
  `resolute`, `amd64v3` pool) serves at 35–55 kB/s for long stretches. Index updates (≈4.6 MB) stay
  fast or at worst ≈30 s; the 63.7 MB package download is the slow part.
- Cache integrity: a restored `.deb` is untrusted until it has been checked against the
  resolved download set. apt 3.2 checks only the size of an already-present archive. A
  same-size corrupted file passes the download stage (`Need to get 0 B`) and fails only later in
  dpkg (probe in Traced premises). The action therefore checks each restored file itself (O2).
  Branch caches are scoped by GitHub, so a feature-branch cache cannot be restored on `master`.
  Anyone who can write `master`'s cache can already change the workflow, so deliberate poisoning
  is out of scope. Only corruption is in scope.
- Non-goal: changing which packages are installed or their versions; changing `release.yml`
  (pinned `ubuntu-24.04` WebKit 2.44 set with a different package list and a 120-minute budget);
  raising any job's `timeout-minutes`.

## Traced premises

- `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` — `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`: `sudo apt-get update`
  then `sudo apt-get install --no-install-recommends -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev
  libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf`. No cache, no bound. Composite steps accept no
  `timeout-minutes`.
- Consumers with `desktop: "true"`: `.github/workflows/test.yml:173` (`frontend`, 90 min) and
  `.github/workflows/test.yml:250` (`rust`, 30 min). `e2e` (test.yml:224) and `frontend-mutation`
  (test.yml:334, mutation.yml:156) call `setup-ci` without `desktop`.
- The same package list is copied verbatim into `.github/workflows/mutation.yml:74-81` (`backend`,
  120 min, scheduled/dispatch only) and `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml:89-97` (Linux leg,
  300 min). Neither uses `setup-ci`.
- Measured step timings on master, 2026-11-06 (`gh api …/runs/<id>/jobs`, job logs in
  `$RUN_TMP/ev/`): `frontend` "Set up repository dependencies" took 20 min 32 s (run 42177925912),
  24 min 14 s (42173575090) and 27 min 59 s (42142451793), against 36–62 s on the others. Every slow
  case is the package download (`Fetched 63.7 MB in 19min 59s (53.1 kB/s)`, `Fetched 49.9 MB in
  23min 11s (35.9 kB/s)`, `Fetched 63.7 MB in 27min 23s (38.7 kB/s)`). `apt-get update` finished in
  0–31 s. On a healthy runner the download is `Fetched 63.7 MB in 4s`.
- `rust` job work after setup: "Run Rust tests with coverage" ends 6–8 min after setup ends, and
  the whole job takes 8–10 min.
- Push skill (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:411-415`): a red job whose failing or timed-out step is
  a setup step (`Set up repository dependencies`, …) gets one `gh run rerun --failed` after the
  run completes. A hang in a later step takes the red-job path.
- `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:276-283` requires actions pinned to a commit SHA only in
  `contents: write` jobs. The jobs touched here are read-only. `pnpm workflows:check` runs
  actionlint 1.7.12 over `.github/workflows/`.
- `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1075-1096` (`ciWorkflowContracts`, run by `gates:routing:test`)
  pins the `setup-ci` composite text, including the literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`.
- Probe 2026-11-06 in a disposable `ubuntu:24.04` container with real apt: `--print-uris` prints
  `'URI' filename size MD5Sum:…`. `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=<dir> --download-only` stores the `.deb`
  there, root-owned 0644. After the file was overwritten with garbage, apt fetched it again
  (`Need to get 106 kB`). With an intact file, apt reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB of archives`.
- Second probe, 2026-11-06, `ubuntu:26.04` (apt 3.2.0): after four bytes were overwritten in a
  cached `.deb` without changing its size, `--download-only` reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB`,
  so the file was not re-verified. `--no-download` install then failed in dpkg
  (`Sub-process /usr/bin/dpkg returned an error code (1)`). With the `.deb` removed, the
  `--no-download` install failed with `E: Unable to fetch some archives` and fetched nothing. In
  the archive directory, apt left `lock` as root 0640 and `partial/` as `_apt` 0700. On 26.04
  the `--print-uris` line also ends in `MD5Sum:<hex>`.
- Third probe, 2026-11-06, `ubuntu:26.04`: with `patchelf_….deb` already in the default
  `/var/cache/apt/archives`, `--print-uris` printed 0 lines. Against an empty
  `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=` directory it printed the 1 line. A stale extra `.deb` in the archive
  directory did not disturb a `--no-download` install.
- Repository Actions cache: 11.86 GB active across 26 entries (`gh api …/actions/cache/usage`),
  so GitHub already evicts least-recently-used entries. A key restored on every push stays recent.

## Approach

### O1 — One implementation of the Linux desktop dependency install
A new local composite action, `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`, owns the package
list and the whole install. `setup-ci`'s desktop branch, `mutation.yml` `backend` and the Linux leg
of `catalog-engines.yml` all call it, so the four-way copy of the package list becomes one copy.
This follows from MANDATE "Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download": left alone,
the two copies outside `setup-ci` would still be uncached and unbounded. Package names and
`--no-install-recommends` stay exactly as they are. The `setup-ci` step that calls the action keeps
the literal condition `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`, which `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`
pins. The `catalog-engines.yml` call keeps `if: runner.os == 'Linux'`. Its `push` and
`pull_request` path filters gain `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/**`, so a later edit to the
action alone still runs the Linux leg. Every `run:` step in the
composite declares `shell: bash`, because composite steps do not inherit a workflow's
`defaults.run.shell`.

### O2 — Downloaded packages come from a cache keyed on exactly what apt would fetch
Order: `apt-get update` → resolve the download set → restore the cache → download only what is
missing → prune → save → install.
- **Download set.** After the update, `apt-get install --print-uris -qq --no-install-recommends
  <packages>` on the runner as booted prints one `'URI' filename size checksum` line per missing
  package. The resolve runs against the same, still empty, archive directory, so the set never
  depends on the runner's default `/var/cache/apt/archives`. The resolve step emits the set
  prefix, the bare fallback prefix and the save key as step outputs, because `actions/cache` takes
  its keys as expressions and runner environment variables are not in that context. The restore's
  primary `key` is the unique save key, which never matches. Its `restore-keys` are the two
  prefixes. The set prefix is `linux-desktop-apt-<VERSION_CODENAME>-<dpkg architecture>-` plus a
  hash of the sorted `filename size checksum` columns. The mirror URI is excluded, so a different
  mirror host yields the same set prefix for the same packages. Restore tries the set prefix
  first, then the bare `linux-desktop-apt-<codename>-<arch>-` prefix. After a security update or
  a runner image bump, the newest older entry still covers most packages.
- **Archive directory.** A world-traversable directory under `/tmp`, created by the runner user,
  is handed to apt as its archive directory for both the download and the install. apt verifies
  every cached file against the signed index and fetches it again on mismatch (threat model).
  Only what is missing or mismatched is downloaded.
- **What the cache holds.** The cache `path` is the `.deb` files only, never the directory. apt
  run as root leaves a root-owned `lock` and an `_apt`-owned 0700 `partial/` there, which
  `actions/cache/save` running as the runner user cannot read.
- **Verify restored files.** Right after the restore, every restored `.deb` whose size or
  checksum differs from its line in the download set is deleted, with one log line naming the
  file and the mismatch, so the download fetches it again. apt alone would accept a same-size
  corrupted file (threat model).
- **Prune.** After the download, `.deb` files whose names are not in the current download set are
  deleted before saving, so carried-forward entries do not grow. The bound is one resolved set,
  ≈64 MB.
- **Save.** Whenever the download fetched at least one file (a cold miss, a fallback-prefix
  restore, or a file the verification deleted), right after the download succeeds and before
  installation. The save key is the set prefix plus the run id and attempt, so it is unique.
  The set-prefix restore picks the newest entry, so a healed set replaces a corrupt one from the
  next run on. A run that downloaded nothing saves nothing. A failed save, including a
  reservation conflict with a parallel job (`frontend` and `rust`), is a warning and never fails
  the job.
- **Fail-open.** The cache is an optimisation. A failed restore, key derivation, verification,
  prune or save degrades to the plain bounded mirror download of O3 and never fails the step.
  Mechanism: the restore and save steps run only when the key output is non-empty and carry
  `continue-on-error: true`. A failing verification runs before the download, so it empties the
  archive directory and everything is downloaded. A failing prune runs after the download, so it
  keeps every file (a stale extra `.deb` does not disturb the install, third probe) and skips the
  save, so an unpruned set is never cached. Either way the step continues. A non-zero exit
  from the `--print-uris` resolve is an apt failure, and the step fails with apt's own error, not
  a stall label. An empty download set with exit 0 means everything is already installed: no cache
  steps run.

### O3 — A stalled download fails as an identified setup step
- **Input.** `linux-desktop-deps` takes `timeout-minutes`. Empty means no bound. Any other value
  that is not a positive number fails the step before any apt call, so a typo or `0` cannot
  silently remove the bound.
- **Shared deadline.** One wall-clock deadline, starting when `apt-get update` starts and carried
  across steps, covers the update and the package download. The restore and verification time in
  between counts against it, but the restore itself is a `uses:` step that `timeout` cannot wrap.
  Its own bound is `actions/cache`'s segment timeout. Each command runs as `sudo timeout --kill-after=<grace> <remaining> apt-get …`, so the
  signal is sent as root and reaches apt and its `_apt` method children. If the remaining time
  reaches zero or less before the download starts, the step fails at once.
- **Install.** The install from the local archive runs with `--no-download`. A missing archive
  then fails fast and by name instead of starting an unbounded mirror fetch.
- **Annotation.** Only a deadline expiry prints the `::error::` annotation: a `timeout` exit (124,
  or 137 after kill) with elapsed time at or past the bound, or remaining time at zero before the
  download starts. An OOM-killed apt (137 before the deadline) is not labelled. It names the mirror stall, the phase (update or download), the elapsed time and the
  bound. Any other apt failure keeps apt's own exit status and output. Either way the step fails
  inside "Set up repository dependencies" (via `setup-ci`), which already matches the push
  skill's setup-step rerun exception. No skill text changes.
- **Who is bounded.** `setup-ci` passes a `desktop-timeout-minutes` input through. Only the `rust`
  job sets it, to **18**. Sizing: 18 min of network time + ≈1 min other setup + ≤10 min observed
  job work ≤ 29 min, under the 30-minute job budget. `frontend` (90 min), `backend` (120) and
  `catalog-engines` (300) absorb the worst observed 28-minute download, so they stay unbounded.
  They are the jobs that populate a cold cache on a slow mirror.
- **Retry.** No in-job retry. A retry would hit the same slow mirror and spend the same budget. The
  push skill's single rerun runs after the whole run completes, so by then `frontend`'s save has
  normally made the rerun a cache hit.

### O4 — Verification
- **Wiring contract (committed).** Extend the existing CI wiring contracts in
  `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (run by `gates:routing:test` inside
  `gates:contract:check`), in its `requireText`/`forbid` style. Checks:
  - the `rust` job passes the literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`;
  - `setup-ci` forwards that input to `linux-desktop-deps` under `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`;
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` and `catalog-engines.yml` call `linux-desktop-deps`;
  - `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` keep no inline `apt-get install` of
    `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev`;
  - inside `linux-desktop-deps`: the restore and save steps exist with `continue-on-error: true`,
    the save step comes before the install step, each of the two apt network commands (update and
    download) uses `sudo timeout --kill-after`, the `--print-uris` resolve and the download pass
    the same `Dir::Cache::Archives` option, and the install uses `--no-download`.

  Each check gets a row in the existing mutated-string rejection table
  (`check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`, the loop after `ciWorkflowContracts`), so it is shown to go red
  when its wiring is reverted. Checks scoped to a step match that step's text, not the whole
  file. `readCiWorkflowTexts` is extended to read `catalog-engines.yml` and the new action.
- **Static.** `pnpm workflows:check` (actionlint and the permission checker) covers
  `.github/workflows/` only, not composite actions. The composite's `run:` bodies are additionally
  checked with `shellcheck`.
- **Real-apt probe (disposable, not committed).** Run the composite's actual `run:` bodies,
  extracted from `action.yml`, as a non-root user with real `sudo` in a disposable `ubuntu:26.04`
  container. Record `sudo --version` and `apt --version`. Pass any proxy to apt through `-o`
  options, because `sudo` drops `http_proxy`. The harness reproduces the `GITHUB_ENV` and
  `GITHUB_OUTPUT` hand-off between steps, so the carried deadline and key outputs are exercised.
  It must show:
  1. the real `--print-uris` format parses into a non-empty download set, and the key does not
     change when only the mirror host differs. With a `.deb` seeded into the default
     `/var/cache/apt/archives`, that package is still in the set;
  2. a cold run downloads, and the saved `.deb` set (the cache `path`) is readable by the
     non-root user;
  3. a second run with that set restored reports `Need to get 0 B` and saves nothing;
  4. a same-size corrupted restored `.deb` is deleted by the verification with its log line,
     fetched again, and the run saves;
  5. the prune removes a stale `.deb`. An injected prune failure keeps the downloaded files,
     skips the save and still installs with exit 0. An injected verification failure and an
     empty key output each lead to a full download and exit 0;
  6. a `.deb` removed between download and install makes the `--no-download` install fail at once
     without a mirror fetch;
  7. stalls are stopped by the `sudo timeout` form with the labelled `::error::` line, in both
     the update phase and the download phase. A deadline already used up before the download
     fails at once with the label. Simulate the stall with a proxy that accepts connections and
     never answers, and keep the probe bound below apt's own 120-second idle timeout;
  8. `timeout-minutes` values `0`, `-1` and `abc` fail before any apt call, `1.5` is accepted, an
     empty value runs unbounded, and a non-timeout apt failure (an unknown package) carries no
     stall label;
  9. a download that fails before the bound (apt killed with SIGKILL, or a proxy that answers 404)
     ends non-zero with no stall label.
- **CI, release gate.**
  - The first `Test` run on the pushed SHA must show the `rust` and `frontend` logs resolving the
    same key and one of them saving it.
  - The next `Test` run (the next push, or `gh workflow run test.yml --ref master -R
    felixabeck/chessriddle-studio`) must show a restore matched on the set prefix (`cache-matched-key`), `Need to get 0 B`
    in both desktop jobs' download step, and no save. The orchestrator confirms this before closing the finding.
    If the two jobs resolve different keys, rerun once before suspecting the wiring, because apt
    picks its `amd64v3` variant pool from the host CPU.
  - The new action file is a frontend-mutation input (`scripts/run-frontend-mutation.mjs:50`
    covers `.github/actions/**`), so the pushed SHA runs the full frontend mutation jobs. That is
    expected and is not a failure of this change.
  - The push also triggers `catalog-engines.yml` (its own path filter): the Linux leg must run the
    action, and the Windows and macOS legs must skip it and stay green.
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` runs only on schedule or dispatch. Its call is proven statically by the
    wiring contract, and functionally by the same action in `rust` and `frontend`. The Sunday
    2026-11-08 run is a follow-up observation, not a closure gate. A dispatch would start the full
    mutation matrix.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (technical, under `full auto`):
- **Question:** cache, bound with retry, or separate test budget? **Chosen:** cache (O2), plus a
  per-job network bound only where the job budget cannot absorb a stall (O3). **Rejected:**
  bound-and-retry alone, because the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled master runs, so retries
  would turn red often and still download 64 MB at ≈40 kB/s. A separate test-step budget or a
  larger job timeout leaves the 20–28 minute download in every slow run and still fails in a test
  step. Swapping mirrors is ruled out because nothing in our evidence shows which mirror is
  healthy, and a hard-coded host would be another observation-pinned URL. A third-party
  apt-cache action is ruled out because `actions/cache` restore/save covers it with an action this
  repository already uses (`mutation.yml`). **Reason:** the measurements in Traced premises.
  **Reversal path:** delete `linux-desktop-deps`'s cache steps; the install falls back to the
  mirror.
- **Question:** unify the copies in `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml`? **Chosen:** yes, O1.
  **Rejected:** caching only `setup-ci`, which leaves two uncached copies of the same list (rule
  11). `release.yml` stays separate because its list and pins differ.

## Risks / open questions

- On a cold cache with a slow mirror, `rust` can still fail at its 18-minute bound. That is the
  intended identified setup failure; the rerun normally hits the cache saved by `frontend`.
- GitHub cache LRU eviction above 10 GB could evict the key during a quiet week. The cost is one
  cold download, so it is a known limit.
- A push that cold-builds Rust (new `Cargo.lock`) and coincides with a download just under 18
  minutes can still exceed the 30-minute job budget in a later step. That needs both a cold Rust
  cache and a cold apt cache on a slow mirror. It is a known limit, not covered by this change.
- `frontend` stays unbounded. A download longer than its remaining budget (more than about 75
  minutes) would still redden it in a later step. The worst observed download is 28 minutes.

## Not part of this task

- `release.yml`'s pinned apt install.
- f-20261106-02 (macOS hosted-runner loss; co-located, not planned in this plan-only run).
- Any change to job `timeout-minutes` or to the push skill's rerun rule.

## Carried to diff review

- CR-1 — Step-scoped checks on `uses:` steps need a step splitter. `workflowSteps`
  (`scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs:166-184`) skips steps without `run:`, and the composite has no
  `jobs:` for `workflowJobs` to parse.
- CR-2 — New rejection-table rows should assert the specific labelled contract, not just any
  throw, where the existing helper allows it (`check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`, the `assert.throws`
  loop).
- CR-3 — Remaining and elapsed time come from the same integer epoch source, so a real exit 124
  at the bound is labelled.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — `linux-desktop-deps` action and its three callers
Files: `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml` (new), `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`,
`.github/workflows/test.yml` (`rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`),
`.github/workflows/mutation.yml` (`backend`), `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml` (Linux leg).
No auth, persistence, concurrency or API surface. Role: normal.
Also `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (O4 wiring contract).
PROOF COMMAND: `pnpm workflows:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm gates:contract:check`,
`shellcheck` over the composite's extracted `run:` bodies, then the O4 real-apt container probe,
reproducing its output. The O4 CI release gate is collected after the push and before the finding
closes.

Plan-level adoptions r2 = 8 (PR-4, PR-11, PR-12, PR-19, PR-25, PR-26, PR-27, PR-28; arbiter nits excluded).

### Round 3 (r3, 2026-11-06) — closure round: review-plan, correctness, error-handling, tests, minimalism
Verdicts: review-plan REVISE · review-correctness REVISE (blocker D1) · review-error-handling APPROVED (should-fix findings) ·
review-tests REVISE · review-minimalism APPROVED. Raw reports: `$RUN_TMP/lens-<lens>-r3.txt`.
Closed by every witness in r3: PR-11, PR-12, PR-19, PR-25, PR-26, PR-27, PR-28. Reopened: PR-4, its second failed closure.

**PR-4 lineage judgment (second failed closure, build §4 escalation).**
- Invariant: a cache-layer failure never fails the step.
- Previous answers: r2 "a failing restore, key derivation, prune or save degrades to the plain download" had no mechanism. r3 "a failing verification or prune empties the archive directory" was wrong for prune. Prune runs after the download, so emptying the directory left nothing for the `--no-download` install (`E: Unable to fetch some archives`, second probe).
- New evidence: third probe — a stale extra `.deb` in the archive directory does not disturb a `--no-download` install.
- Judgment: simplest correct mechanism, by phase. A failure before the download (verification, key) empties the directory and the download refills it. A failure after the download (prune) keeps every file and skips the save. Restore and save are guarded on a non-empty key with `continue-on-error: true`.
- Changes: O2 Fail-open, probe item 5. The round-4 review-plan prompt carries this as a focused fresh-context judgment request.

### plan-r5.md

# Plan: Cache and bound the Linux desktop apt install in CI (f-20261106-07)

## Goal

A slow Ubuntu mirror no longer turns the `rust` job red inside a test step. In the normal case,
the WebKitGTK build dependencies come from a GitHub Actions cache instead of the mirror. When the
download does stall, it fails inside the setup step, which matches the push skill's existing
runner-infrastructure rerun exception.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261106-07:

> * **Where:** `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` (uncached `sudo apt-get update` + `apt-get install` of the WebKitGTK desktop build dependencies), `.github/workflows/test.yml:244` (`rust` job `timeout-minutes: 30`, which covers setup and tests together).
> * **Defect:** On the push of 17b782bb (run 42167043743, `rust` job 127725818339) the apt step fetched 63.7 MB in 22 min 52 s (46.4 kB/s) from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` (05:13:03 → 05:36:00Z). Rust setup, clippy and the coverage build then left about 4 minutes for `pnpm test:coverage:backend`, and the job was cancelled at the 30-minute limit in "Run Rust tests with coverage" while tests were still passing. The same job on the two preceding master runs took 8 min 22 s and 8 min 36 s. The pushed diff changed no Rust code. Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download, so a mirror stall becomes a red repository-check step instead of an identified setup failure, and the push skill's setup-step rerun exception does not match it.
> * **Failure scenario:** a mirror slowdown on any push reddens `rust` (or another desktop job) at its timeout inside a test step. `pnpm ci:remote:check` then refuses the next push until someone diagnoses the log and reruns it.
> * **Open question:** cache the desktop apt packages (for example, an apt package cache keyed on the package list), bound the apt step with its own `timeout-minutes` and one retry so a stall fails as a setup step, or give the test step its own budget separate from setup.

## Threat model and non-goals

- External condition: the hosted runner's Ubuntu mirror (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, Ubuntu 26.04
  `resolute`, `amd64v3` pool) serves at 35–55 kB/s for long stretches. Index updates (≈4.6 MB) stay
  fast or at worst ≈30 s; the 63.7 MB package download is the slow part.
- Cache integrity: a restored `.deb` is untrusted until it has been checked against the
  resolved download set. apt 3.2 checks only the size of an already-present archive. A
  same-size corrupted file passes the download stage (`Need to get 0 B`) and fails only later in
  dpkg (probe in Traced premises). The action therefore checks each restored file itself (O2).
  Branch caches are scoped by GitHub, so a feature-branch cache cannot be restored on `master`.
  Anyone who can write `master`'s cache can already change the workflow, so deliberate poisoning
  is out of scope. Only corruption is in scope.
- Non-goal: changing which packages are installed or their versions; changing `release.yml`
  (pinned `ubuntu-24.04` WebKit 2.44 set with a different package list and a 120-minute budget);
  raising any job's `timeout-minutes`.

## Traced premises

- `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml:31-41` — `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`: `sudo apt-get update`
  then `sudo apt-get install --no-install-recommends -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev
  libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf`. No cache, no bound. Composite steps accept no
  `timeout-minutes`.
- Consumers with `desktop: "true"`: `.github/workflows/test.yml:173` (`frontend`, 90 min) and
  `.github/workflows/test.yml:250` (`rust`, 30 min). `e2e` (test.yml:224) and `frontend-mutation`
  (test.yml:334, mutation.yml:156) call `setup-ci` without `desktop`.
- The same package list is copied verbatim into `.github/workflows/mutation.yml:74-81` (`backend`,
  120 min, scheduled/dispatch only) and `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml:89-97` (Linux leg,
  300 min). Neither uses `setup-ci`.
- Measured step timings on master, 2026-11-06 (`gh api …/runs/<id>/jobs`, job logs in
  `$RUN_TMP/ev/`): `frontend` "Set up repository dependencies" took 20 min 32 s (run 42177925912),
  24 min 14 s (42173575090) and 27 min 59 s (42142451793), against 36–62 s on the others. Every slow
  case is the package download (`Fetched 63.7 MB in 19min 59s (53.1 kB/s)`, `Fetched 49.9 MB in
  23min 11s (35.9 kB/s)`, `Fetched 63.7 MB in 27min 23s (38.7 kB/s)`). `apt-get update` finished in
  0–31 s. On a healthy runner the download is `Fetched 63.7 MB in 4s`.
- `rust` job work after setup: "Run Rust tests with coverage" ends 6–8 min after setup ends, and
  the whole job takes 8–10 min.
- Push skill (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:411-415`): a red job whose failing or timed-out step is
  a setup step (`Set up repository dependencies`, …) gets one `gh run rerun --failed` after the
  run completes. A hang in a later step takes the red-job path.
- `scripts/check-workflow-permissions.mjs:276-283` requires actions pinned to a commit SHA only in
  `contents: write` jobs. The jobs touched here are read-only. `pnpm workflows:check` runs
  actionlint 1.7.12 over `.github/workflows/`.
- `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:1075-1096` (`ciWorkflowContracts`, run by `gates:routing:test`)
  pins the `setup-ci` composite text, including the literal `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`.
- Probe 2026-11-06 in a disposable `ubuntu:24.04` container with real apt: `--print-uris` prints
  `'URI' filename size MD5Sum:…`. `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=<dir> --download-only` stores the `.deb`
  there, root-owned 0644. After the file was overwritten with garbage, apt fetched it again
  (`Need to get 106 kB`). With an intact file, apt reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB of archives`.
- Second probe, 2026-11-06, `ubuntu:26.04` (apt 3.2.0): after four bytes were overwritten in a
  cached `.deb` without changing its size, `--download-only` reported `Need to get 0 B/106 kB`,
  so the file was not re-verified. `--no-download` install then failed in dpkg
  (`Sub-process /usr/bin/dpkg returned an error code (1)`). With the `.deb` removed, the
  `--no-download` install failed with `E: Unable to fetch some archives` and fetched nothing. In
  the archive directory, apt left `lock` as root 0640 and `partial/` as `_apt` 0700. On 26.04
  the `--print-uris` line also ends in `MD5Sum:<hex>`.
- Third probe, 2026-11-06, `ubuntu:26.04`: with `patchelf_….deb` already in the default
  `/var/cache/apt/archives`, `--print-uris` printed 0 lines. Against an empty
  `-o Dir::Cache::Archives=` directory it printed the 1 line. A stale extra `.deb` in the archive
  directory did not disturb a `--no-download` install.
- Repository Actions cache: 11.86 GB active across 26 entries (`gh api …/actions/cache/usage`),
  so GitHub already evicts least-recently-used entries. A key restored on every push stays recent.

## Approach

### O1 — One implementation of the Linux desktop dependency install
A new local composite action, `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml`, owns the package
list and the whole install. `setup-ci`'s desktop branch, `mutation.yml` `backend` and the Linux leg
of `catalog-engines.yml` all call it, so the four-way copy of the package list becomes one copy.
This follows from MANDATE "Nothing in the workflow bounds or caches the apt download": left alone,
the two copies outside `setup-ci` would still be uncached and unbounded. Package names and
`--no-install-recommends` stay exactly as they are. The `setup-ci` step that calls the action keeps
the literal condition `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`, which `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`
pins. The `catalog-engines.yml` call keeps `if: runner.os == 'Linux'`. Its `push` and
`pull_request` path filters gain `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/**`, so a later edit to the
action alone still runs the Linux leg. Every `run:` step in the
composite declares `shell: bash`, because composite steps do not inherit a workflow's
`defaults.run.shell`.

### O2 — Downloaded packages come from a cache keyed on exactly what apt would fetch
Order: `apt-get update` → resolve the download set → restore the cache → download only what is
missing → prune → save → install.
- **Download set.** After the update, `apt-get install --print-uris -qq --no-install-recommends
  <packages>` on the runner as booted prints one `'URI' filename size checksum` line per missing
  package. The resolve runs against the same, still empty, archive directory, so the set never
  depends on the runner's default `/var/cache/apt/archives`. The resolve step emits the set
  prefix, the bare fallback prefix and the save key as step outputs, because `actions/cache` takes
  its keys as expressions and runner environment variables are not in that context. The restore's
  primary `key` is the save key. It matches only when a parallel job of the same run (`frontend`
  or `rust`) has already saved, which is benign. Its `restore-keys` are the two prefixes. The set prefix is `linux-desktop-apt-<VERSION_CODENAME>-<dpkg architecture>-` plus a
  hash of the sorted `filename size checksum` columns. The mirror URI is excluded, so a different
  mirror host yields the same set prefix for the same packages. Restore tries the set prefix
  first, then the bare `linux-desktop-apt-<codename>-<arch>-` prefix. After a security update or
  a runner image bump, the newest older entry still covers most packages.
- **Archive directory.** One fixed, world-traversable path under `/tmp` (for example
  `/tmp/linux-desktop-apt-archives`), created by the runner user. It is never a random `mktemp`
  path, because `actions/cache` derives the entry version from the `path` string.
  The directory is handed to apt as its archive directory for both the download and the install. apt verifies
  every cached file against the signed index and fetches it again on mismatch (threat model).
  Only what is missing or mismatched is downloaded.
- **What the cache holds.** The cache `path` is the `.deb` files only, never the directory. apt
  run as root leaves a root-owned `lock` and an `_apt`-owned 0700 `partial/` there, which
  `actions/cache/save` running as the runner user cannot read.
- **Verify restored files.** Right after the restore, every restored `.deb` whose size or
  checksum differs from its line in the download set is deleted, with one log line naming the
  file and the mismatch, so the download fetches it again. apt alone would accept a same-size
  corrupted file (threat model).
- **Prune.** After the download, `.deb` files whose names are not in the current download set are
  deleted before saving, so carried-forward entries do not grow. The bound is one resolved set,
  ≈64 MB.
- **Save.** Whenever the download fetched at least one file (a cold miss, a fallback-prefix
  restore, or a file the verification deleted), right after the download succeeds and before
  installation. The save key is the set prefix plus the run id and attempt, so it is unique.
  The set-prefix restore picks the newest entry, so a healed set replaces a corrupt one from the
  next run on. A run that downloaded nothing saves nothing. A failed save, including a
  reservation conflict with a parallel job (`frontend` and `rust`), is a warning and never fails
  the job.
- **Fail-open.** The cache is an optimisation. A failed restore, key derivation, verification,
  prune or save degrades to the plain bounded mirror download of O3 and never fails the step.
  Mechanism: the restore and save steps run only when the key output is non-empty and carry
  `continue-on-error: true`. A failing verification runs before the download, so it empties the
  archive directory and everything is downloaded. A failing prune runs after the download, so it
  keeps every file (a stale extra `.deb` does not disturb the install, third probe) and skips the
  save, so an unpruned set is never cached. Either way the step continues. Key derivation inside
  the resolve step is guarded separately from the `--print-uris` call. A derivation failure
  empties the key output, while the apt exit status still fails the step.
- **Every degradation is visible.** Each fail-open branch (key derivation, restore, verification,
  prune, save) prints one `::warning::` line naming the stage and the reason. A run that degraded
  never looks like a clean miss in the log.
- **Save guard state.** The download step emits whether it fetched at least one file, and the
  prune step emits whether it succeeded, both as step outputs. The save step's `if:` requires a
  non-empty key, fetched = true and prune ok = true. A missing or undetermined value means do
  not save. A non-zero exit
  from the `--print-uris` resolve is an apt failure, and the step fails with apt's own error, not
  a stall label. An empty download set with exit 0 means everything is already installed: no cache
  steps run.

### O3 — A stalled download fails as an identified setup step
- **Input.** `linux-desktop-deps` takes `timeout-minutes`. Empty means no bound. Any other value
  that is not a positive number fails the step before any apt call, so a typo or `0` cannot
  silently remove the bound.
- **Shared deadline.** One wall-clock deadline, starting when `apt-get update` starts and carried
  across steps, covers the update and the package download. The restore and verification time in
  between counts against it, but the restore itself is a `uses:` step that `timeout` cannot wrap.
  Its own bound is `actions/cache`'s segment timeout. Each command runs as `sudo timeout --kill-after=<grace> <remaining> apt-get …`, so the
  signal is sent as root and reaches apt and its `_apt` method children. If the remaining time
  reaches zero or less before the download starts, the step fails at once.
- **Install.** The install from the local archive runs with `--no-download`. A missing archive
  then fails fast and by name instead of starting an unbounded mirror fetch.
- **Annotation.** Only a deadline expiry prints the `::error::` annotation: a `timeout` exit (124,
  or 137 after kill) with elapsed time at or past the bound, or remaining time at zero before the
  download starts. An OOM-killed apt (137 before the deadline) is not labelled. It names the mirror stall, the phase (update or download), the elapsed time and the
  bound. Any other apt failure keeps apt's own exit status and output. Either way the step fails
  inside "Set up repository dependencies" (via `setup-ci`), which already matches the push
  skill's setup-step rerun exception. No skill text changes.
- **Who is bounded.** `setup-ci` passes a `desktop-timeout-minutes` input through. Only the `rust`
  job sets it, to **18**. Sizing: 18 min of network time + ≈1 min other setup + ≤10 min observed
  job work ≤ 29 min, under the 30-minute job budget. `frontend` (90 min), `backend` (120) and
  `catalog-engines` (300) absorb the worst observed 28-minute download, so they stay unbounded.
  They are the jobs that populate a cold cache on a slow mirror.
- **Retry.** No in-job retry. A retry would hit the same slow mirror and spend the same budget. The
  push skill's single rerun runs after the whole run completes, so by then `frontend`'s save has
  normally made the rerun a cache hit.

### O4 — Verification
- **Wiring contract (committed).** Extend the existing CI wiring contracts in
  `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (run by `gates:routing:test` inside
  `gates:contract:check`), in its `requireText`/`forbid` style. Checks:
  - the `rust` job passes the literal `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`;
  - `setup-ci` forwards that input to `linux-desktop-deps` under `if: inputs.desktop == 'true'`;
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` and `catalog-engines.yml` call `linux-desktop-deps`;
  - `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml` keep no inline `apt-get install` of
    `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev`;
  - inside `linux-desktop-deps`: the restore and save steps exist with `continue-on-error: true`
    and their step-scoped `if:` guards (restore: key non-empty; save: key non-empty, fetched and
    prune ok, as named in O2 "Save guard state"),
    the save step comes before the install step, each of the two apt network commands (update and
    download) uses `sudo timeout --kill-after`, the `--print-uris` resolve and the download pass
    the same `Dir::Cache::Archives` option, and the install uses `--no-download`.

  Each check gets a row in the existing mutated-string rejection table
  (`check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`, the loop after `ciWorkflowContracts`), so it is shown to go red
  when its wiring is reverted. Checks scoped to a step match that step's text, not the whole
  file. `readCiWorkflowTexts` is extended to read `catalog-engines.yml` and the new action.
- **Static.** `pnpm workflows:check` (actionlint and the permission checker) covers
  `.github/workflows/` only, not composite actions. The composite's `run:` bodies are additionally
  checked with `shellcheck`.
- **Real-apt probe (disposable, not committed).** Run the composite's actual `run:` bodies,
  extracted from `action.yml`, as a non-root user with real `sudo` in a disposable `ubuntu:26.04`
  container. Record `sudo --version` and `apt --version`. Pass any proxy to apt through `-o`
  options, because `sudo` drops `http_proxy`. The harness reproduces the `GITHUB_ENV` and
  `GITHUB_OUTPUT` hand-off between steps, so the carried deadline and key outputs are exercised.
  It must show:
  1. the real `--print-uris` format parses into a non-empty download set, and the key does not
     change when only the mirror host differs. With a `.deb` seeded into the default
     `/var/cache/apt/archives`, that package is still in the set;
  2. a cold run downloads, and the saved `.deb` set (the cache `path`) is readable by the
     non-root user;
  3. a second run, in a fresh container with that set restored, reports `Need to get 0 B` and saves
     nothing;
  4. a same-size corrupted restored `.deb` is deleted by the verification with its log line,
     fetched again, and the run saves;
  5. the prune removes a stale `.deb`. An injected prune failure keeps the downloaded files,
     skips the save and still installs with exit 0. An injected verification failure and an
     injected key-derivation failure (a broken hash command, not a hand-set empty output) each
     lead to a full download and exit 0. Every injected failure prints its `::warning::` line, and
     the guard outputs read "do not save" where required;
  6. a `.deb` removed between download and install makes the `--no-download` install fail at once
     without a mirror fetch;
  7. stalls are stopped by the `sudo timeout` form with the labelled `::error::` line, in both
     the update phase and the download phase. A deadline already used up before the download
     fails at once with the label. Simulate the stall with a proxy that accepts connections and
     never answers, and keep the probe bound below apt's own 120-second idle timeout;
  8. `timeout-minutes` values `0`, `-1` and `abc` fail before any apt call with a message naming the
     value, `1.5` is accepted, an
     empty value runs unbounded, and a non-timeout apt failure (an unknown package) carries no
     stall label;
  9. a download that fails before the bound (apt killed with SIGKILL, or a proxy that answers 404)
     ends non-zero with no stall label.
- **CI, release gate.**
  - The first `Test` run on the pushed SHA must show the `rust` and `frontend` logs resolving the
    same key and one of them saving it.
  - The next `Test` run (the next push, or `gh workflow run test.yml --ref master -R
    felixabeck/chessriddle-studio`) must show a restore matched on the set prefix (`cache-matched-key`), `Need to get 0 B`
    in both desktop jobs' download step, and no save. On the first run one job may report
    `cache-hit` true when the other job saved first. That is benign. The orchestrator confirms this before closing the finding.
    If the two jobs resolve different keys, rerun once before suspecting the wiring, because apt
    picks its `amd64v3` variant pool from the host CPU.
  - The new action file is a frontend-mutation input (`scripts/run-frontend-mutation.mjs:50`
    covers `.github/actions/**`), so the pushed SHA runs the full frontend mutation jobs. That is
    expected and is not a failure of this change.
  - The push also triggers `catalog-engines.yml` (its own path filter): the Linux leg must run the
    action, and the Windows and macOS legs must skip it and stay green.
  - `mutation.yml` `backend` runs only on schedule or dispatch. Its call is proven statically by the
    wiring contract, and functionally by the same action in `rust` and `frontend`. The Sunday
    2026-11-08 run is a follow-up observation, not a closure gate. A dispatch would start the full
    mutation matrix.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (technical, under `full auto`):
- **Question:** cache, bound with retry, or separate test budget? **Chosen:** cache (O2), plus a
  per-job network bound only where the job budget cannot absorb a stall (O3). **Rejected:**
  bound-and-retry alone, because the mirror was slow in 3 of 5 sampled master runs, so retries
  would turn red often and still download 64 MB at ≈40 kB/s. A separate test-step budget or a
  larger job timeout leaves the 20–28 minute download in every slow run and still fails in a test
  step. Swapping mirrors is ruled out because nothing in our evidence shows which mirror is
  healthy, and a hard-coded host would be another observation-pinned URL. A third-party
  apt-cache action is ruled out because `actions/cache` restore/save covers it with an action this
  repository already uses (`mutation.yml`). **Reason:** the measurements in Traced premises.
  **Reversal path:** delete `linux-desktop-deps`'s cache steps; the install falls back to the
  mirror.
- **Question:** unify the copies in `mutation.yml` and `catalog-engines.yml`? **Chosen:** yes, O1.
  **Rejected:** caching only `setup-ci`, which leaves two uncached copies of the same list (rule
  11). `release.yml` stays separate because its list and pins differ.

## Risks / open questions

- On a cold cache with a slow mirror, `rust` can still fail at its 18-minute bound. That is the
  intended identified setup failure; the rerun normally hits the cache saved by `frontend`.
- GitHub cache LRU eviction above 10 GB could evict the key during a quiet week. The cost is one
  cold download, so it is a known limit.
- A push that cold-builds Rust (new `Cargo.lock`) and coincides with a download just under 18
  minutes can still exceed the 30-minute job budget in a later step. That needs both a cold Rust
  cache and a cold apt cache on a slow mirror. It is a known limit, not covered by this change.
- `frontend` stays unbounded. A download longer than its remaining budget (more than about 75
  minutes) would still redden it in a later step. The worst observed download is 28 minutes.

## Not part of this task

- `release.yml`'s pinned apt install.
- f-20261106-02 (macOS hosted-runner loss; co-located, not planned in this plan-only run).
- Any change to job `timeout-minutes` or to the push skill's rerun rule.

## Carried to diff review

- CR-1 — Step-scoped checks on `uses:` steps need a step splitter. `workflowSteps`
  (`scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs:166-184`) skips steps without `run:`, and the composite has no
  `jobs:` for `workflowJobs` to parse.
- CR-2 — New rejection-table rows should assert the specific labelled contract, not just any
  throw, where the existing helper allows it (`check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`, the `assert.throws`
  loop).
- CR-3 — Remaining and elapsed time come from the same integer epoch source, so a real exit 124
  at the bound is labelled.
- CR-4 — The verification reads the checksum type from its prefix (`MD5Sum:` today, possibly
  `SHA256:`) rather than assuming one hash.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — `linux-desktop-deps` action and its three callers
Files: `.github/actions/linux-desktop-deps/action.yml` (new), `.github/actions/setup-ci/action.yml`,
`.github/workflows/test.yml` (`rust` job passes `desktop-timeout-minutes: "18"`),
`.github/workflows/mutation.yml` (`backend`), `.github/workflows/catalog-engines.yml` (Linux leg).
No auth, persistence, concurrency or API surface. Role: normal.
Also `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs` (O4 wiring contract).
PROOF COMMAND: `pnpm workflows:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm gates:contract:check`,
`shellcheck` over the composite's extracted `run:` bodies, then the O4 real-apt container probe,
reproducing its output. The O4 CI release gate is collected after the push and before the finding
closes.

**PR-4 lineage judgment (second failed closure, build §4 escalation).**
- Invariant: a cache-layer failure never fails the step.
- Previous answers: r2 "a failing restore, key derivation, prune or save degrades to the plain download" had no mechanism. r3 "a failing verification or prune empties the archive directory" was wrong for prune. Prune runs after the download, so emptying the directory left nothing for the `--no-download` install (`E: Unable to fetch some archives`, second probe).
- New evidence: third probe — a stale extra `.deb` in the archive directory does not disturb a `--no-download` install.
- Judgment: simplest correct mechanism, by phase. A failure before the download (verification, key) empties the directory and the download refills it. A failure after the download (prune) keeps every file and skips the save. Restore and save are guarded on a non-empty key with `continue-on-error: true`.
- Changes: O2 Fail-open, probe item 5. The round-4 review-plan prompt carries this as a focused fresh-context judgment request.
